Until that's built, the new content border also encloses Header on those four screens, which web's actual DOM structure doesn't do. Verified live on Home: the result reads as one coherent bordered frame, not a visibly broken composition — acceptable as an interim state, but explicitly not the correct final structure. Finishing Phase 1 is what resolves this correctly, not a workaround here.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live on Home (dark theme, real data) — tighter horizontal margins, the new border/radius/padding, and Header's continued full legibility and tappability inside the bordered frame all confirmed before this entry was written.

## Stage 1, item 4 (Home): holistic side-by-side pass

**Context:** a full-product comparison (not values in isolation) surfaced two things a values-only pass had missed, plus one previously-flagged item still outstanding.

**Correction to an earlier, unverified claim:** the prior card-parity entry described web's loading state as showing "shimmering card-shaped placeholders." That was never actually confirmed against source. Reading `ui/skeleton/skeleton.module.scss` directly now: there is no `@keyframes` or `animation` property anywhere in the file. Web's skeleton is fully static — mobile's existing static (no-shimmer) skeleton was already correct; the earlier written description of it was not.

**Measured precisely from `skeleton.tsx`/`skeleton.module.scss`'s `NewsSkeleton`/`.news` rules (not estimated):** the skeleton is not a loading version of the real card — it's visually distinct on web itself. A solid `--skeleton-wrapper-bg` fill (no border at all, unlike the real card's border-only treatment), centered avatar (32×32, **square, no border-radius** — not circular like the real `Avatar`) and name (90×19), a single combined "Finished + date" bar (200×16, one shape, not two separate texts), then **6** left-aligned exercise-line bars at 80% width (mobile previously showed only 2). All placeholder shapes use a second, distinct `--skeleton-bg` tone. Card-level row-gap 16px, padding 8px.

**Implementation:** added `skeletonBg`/`skeletonWrapperBg` to `theme.ts` (`#fff`/`#d0d4dd` light, `#2e364a`/`#1d273f` dark — exact hex values read from `variables.scss`, a dedicated palette distinct from every other existing token, not approximated). Rebuilt `HomeCardSkeleton` in `src/app/(app)/index.tsx` to match the measured structure exactly: solid wrapper fill, centered header, one combined "Finished" bar, 6 left-aligned line bars.

**Also fixed — a discrepancy flagged but not corrected during the earlier card-parity pass:** the real card's own `gap` (between its header block and exercise-list block) was `Spacing.two` (8px); web's `historyCard{row-gap:16px}` measures 16px. Corrected to `Spacing.three`.

**Found and explicitly not yet acted on — needs a decision, not a unilateral call:** web's `TrainingHistories` has no empty-state branch at all — confirmed by reading the component's full return path: if the history list is empty, it renders nothing, no message. Mobile currently shows "No activity yet." Reproducing web exactly here would mean deleting a working, helpful message in favor of a blank screen with no explanation — flagged rather than silently changed either way.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. The real card's gap change was verified live (Home, dark theme, real data). The skeleton's correctness was verified by tracing every value against source and against RN's rendered style output rather than a live screenshot — the actual network request completes too quickly to reliably capture the loading state on this device/connection.

**Follow-up per the new Stage 2 exception rule (established after this item shipped):** the empty-state text above stays as a deliberate, small kept improvement — it doesn't conflict with web's structure, doesn't touch the shell/navigation, and web's silence here isn't a design choice worth copying. Logged as a Stage 2 review item rather than left as an unresolved question.

## Stage 1, item 4 (Programs): full-screen holistic pass

**Context:** audited `app/training/components/programs/programs.tsx`/`.module.scss` and `ProgramItem.tsx` directly — web's Programs page (reached via `/training/[id]`, redirected to from `/training`) turned out structurally different from mobile's in several ways a values-only pass wouldn't have caught.

**Measured directly from source:**
- Web has a page title mobile lacked entirely: `<h2 className="title">` — `base.scss`'s `.title` resolves to 20px/weight 500/centered (confirmed by reading both of the two same-named rules in the file and resolving the cascade, not assuming either alone) — English copy "My programs" (`lib/languages.ts`), 16px margin-bottom.
- The create action (`.createLink`) is a fixed 40px-tall, centered container, rendered **unconditionally** right after the title — not duplicated between empty and non-empty states the way mobile's was. Copy is "Create new program" (mobile said "Create Program").
- `ProgramItem`: 8px padding (mobile was 16), centered title+subtitle (mobile was left-aligned in a space-between row), a 4px gap between title and subtitle (`span{margin-bottom:4px}`, mobile was 2px), and a **32×32 arrow icon absolutely positioned at `right:32px`, vertically centered** — not a small 16px trailing chevron inline in a flex row. Icon color confirmed against the actual `linkToWhite.svg`/`linkToDark.svg` source: `#fff`/`#19355A`, both exact `theme.text` matches — mobile was using `theme.textSecondary` at 16px.
- `.programList{row-gap:8px}` — already matched mobile's existing `Spacing.two`, no change needed.
- Empty state: a plain, non-centered paragraph, no repeated button (the one create action already sits above, unconditionally). English copy "You don't have any programs yet" — no trailing period (mobile had one).

**A real information-architecture finding, not a value:** web's Programs page has no "browse global programs" entry point anywhere on it — Complexes is reached via header-level secondary navigation (`HeaderNavigation`), which mobile doesn't have yet (Navigation is item 5 on the priority list, not yet started). Removing mobile's existing inline "Browse Global Programs →" link would leave Complexes completely unreachable until that work happens. Kept in place under the new Stage 2 exception rule — logged as a review item, not a silent deviation from web.

**Implementation:** `src/app/(app)/programs/index.tsx` restructured to match — title, then the unconditional create button, then the (kept) Complexes link, then loading/error/empty/list. `src/components/program-card.tsx` rebuilt: centered text, 8px padding, absolutely-positioned 32×32 arrow icon at `theme.text`.

**Technical finding — not web-related, a real React Native quirk:** `SymbolView`'s (`expo-symbols`) Android-rendered view doesn't reliably apply an absolute-position style passed directly to it — the icon rendered in normal document flow instead of at its intended position. Fixed by wrapping `SymbolView` in a plain `View` carrying the positioning style, with `SymbolView` sized normally inside it. Worth remembering for any other absolutely-positioned `SymbolView` usage in this codebase.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live on the Android emulator (dark theme, real data): title, unconditional create button, kept Complexes link, and the program card's centered text and correctly-positioned arrow icon all confirmed after fixing the `SymbolView` positioning issue.

## Stage 1, item 4 (Explore): full-screen holistic pass — largest structural change so far

**Context:** this is the screen the earlier live device audit flagged as the biggest un-tracked structural gap — web's exercise browser (`app/training/wiki/`) is a genuinely different composition from mobile's Explore, not a styling variant of the same layout.

**Measured directly from `wiki.tsx`/`wiki.module.scss`/`wikiNav.tsx`/`Exercise.tsx`, not estimated:**
- A page title mobile lacked (`<h2 className="pageTitle">Wiki</h2>` — `base.scss`'s `.pageTitle`: 18px, centered, 16px margin-bottom — a different value from Programs' `.title`, 20px; confirmed by reading both rules rather than assuming they'd match).
- `.content` is a **row** (`column-gap:12px`, not in the existing spacing scale — used exactly, not rounded), not a vertical stack: a narrow (`max-width:78px`) independently-scrolling muscle-group rail beside an independently-scrolling exercise list.
- `wikiNav.tsx`'s tabs: bordered 64×64 squares (not mobile's previous horizontal text-only pills), 1px border, 4px radius, a 32×32 icon above a 12px label, 16px row-gap between tabs, active tab filled with `--submit-bg` (`#fff` light / `#203045` dark — no existing token matched this exactly).
- `.exercise`: a **bare row** — no border, no fill, no radius at all (mobile previously rendered each exercise as a filled, rounded card).
- Web has no search bar on this page at all — filtering is by muscle group only.

**Asset sourcing:** copied all 16 muscle-group icon files (8 groups × light/dark) verbatim from web's `public/musclesIcons/` into `assets/images/muscle-groups/`, checksum-verified, same discipline as the brand logo sourcing. Note the counter-intuitive naming `wikiNav.tsx` itself uses: the `*Light` file is shown in **dark** theme (it's a light-colored icon meant to sit on a dark background) — preserved exactly, not "corrected."

**Kept per the Stage 2 review rule:** the search bar (`ExerciseSearchBar`) — doesn't conflict with web's structure, doesn't touch shell/navigation, adds a capability rather than removing one.

**Implementation:** `src/components/muscle-group-filter.tsx` rebuilt entirely — vertical `ScrollView` rail of bordered icon+label tabs, replacing the horizontal pill row. `src/app/(app)/explore/index.tsx` restructured into a row (`MuscleGroupFilter` + `FlatList`) instead of a single column; exercise rows stripped of all card chrome to match `.exercise`'s bare styling.

**Debugging note, for the record:** mid-implementation, tab labels appeared to render cut off and mispositioned across several Fast-Refresh reloads, including after explicit style fixes. A UI-accessibility-tree dump (`uiautomator`) showed the actual layout bounds were already correct at that point — the visible bug was a stale Fast Refresh paint, not a real layout defect. Confirmed by a full app relaunch (force-stop + restart), which rendered correctly immediately. Documented so this doesn't get mistaken for a recurring issue: if a style change doesn't visually take effect despite Fast Refresh reporting success, a full relaunch is the next diagnostic step before assuming the code is wrong.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live on the Android emulator after a full relaunch (dark theme, real data): title, kept search bar, the two-column layout with independently-scrolling rail and list, correctly-wrapped tab labels, and bare exercise rows all confirmed.

## Stage 1, item 4 (Profile): full-screen holistic pass

**Context:** audited `Profile.client.tsx`/`Friends.tsx`/`profiles.module.scss` directly. Mobile's Profile header was a centered column (avatar, then name/email/date stacked and centered below it) — web's is a left-aligned row, structurally different, not a styling variant.

**Measured directly from source, not estimated:**
- `.profile__header`: a row (`column-gap:16px`), avatar left, text stack right — not centered.
- Avatar `img{border-radius:4px}` — a **square** (barely rounded), not circular, specific to this page. Web isn't consistent about avatar shape across pages (Home's feed avatar CSS has no radius rule at all), so this is scoped to Profile only, not a change to the shared convention — flagged for the Shell Review rather than assumed to apply everywhere.
- Username: 18px, 8px vertical margin (`p{font-size:18px; margin:8px 0}`).
- Joined date: `{new Date(profile.created_at).toLocaleString()}` — full locale date+time, no "Joined" prefix (mobile had one), dimmed via `opacity:0.4` (mobile used a `textSecondary` color swap — a different dimming mechanism entirely).
- A "Programs" link mobile had no equivalent of at all: bold (600), underlined, 16px, full opacity, linking to that user's programs (web: `/training/{id}`; mobile's equivalent destination is the Programs tab).
- A settings gear icon, absolutely positioned top-right (`right:4px; top:4px`), own-profile only — mobile had no icon-based Settings entry point on this screen at all, only a text row further down and the Header menu. Icon color confirmed against the actual `settings.svg`/`settingsDark.svg` source: `#fff`/`#19355A`, both exact `theme.text` matches, same pattern as every other icon checked this way.

**Implementation:** `src/components/avatar.tsx` gained an additive, optional `radius` prop (default unchanged — `size / 2`, circular, exactly as every other call site already relies on) so Profile could pass `radius={4}` without touching Avatar's existing behavior anywhere else. `src/app/(app)/profile/index.tsx` restructured to the left-aligned row, with the settings icon, date format, and Programs link added to match.

**Deferred, not silently skipped:** `FriendsPreview`'s own exact measurements (`Friends.tsx`/`.friends` rules: 40×40 avatars, wrapping layout capped at 180px height with overflow hidden rather than mobile's fixed 3-item grid) weren't reconciled in this pass — the test account has zero friends, so there was nothing to verify live against, and re-deriving correctness from source alone without a live check didn't meet this project's own verification bar. Flagged explicitly for the upcoming Stage 1 Shell Review rather than left unmentioned.

**Real bug found during testing, explicitly out of scope for this item:** repeated full-relaunch testing (adopted after the Explore stale-paint lesson) surfaced the same expo-router cold-start remount race already diagnosed and fixed for `profile/friends.tsx` in Sprint 38 ("Can't perform a React state update on a component that hasn't mounted yet") — appearing here because forced cold starts hit that code path far more often than normal navigation would. Not caused by this pass's changes (no fetch/effect logic was touched), and not a web-parity issue — a pre-existing mobile-only race condition. Not fixed here; flagged for its own future task.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live after a full relaunch (dark theme, real data): left-aligned header, square avatar radius, full-locale date, Programs link, and the settings icon all confirmed rendering as measured.

## Stage 1: Header shell completion — logo, hamburger glyph, workspace color, friend requests

**Context:** the Shell Review above was judged incomplete — remaining differences were concentrated in the Header and Navigation rather than the content screens. Four specific items were named: the logo isn't clickable, the hamburger icon's shape differs, Header/overall colors still differ, and the friend-request icon/badge is still missing from the Header. All four addressed here; Navigation remains next.

**1. Logo → Home, missing since it was first flagged in an earlier source audit and never actually fixed:** `header.tsx`'s brand block is now wrapped in a `Pressable` (`router.push('/')`), matching web's `Link href="/"` around the same block. Verified live by navigating to Profile, tapping the logo, and confirming it lands back on Home.

**2. Hamburger icon reproduced as web's actual glyph, not a platform symbol approximation:** read `menuMob.svg`'s raw path (`M11 17H19M5 12H19M11 7H19`, 24×24 viewBox, stroke-width 2, round caps) directly — it's **not** a symmetric hamburger. The top and bottom bars are right-aligned and half the width of the middle bar. `SF Symbol`/Material `menu` icons render a standard symmetric hamburger, which is a real, visible shape mismatch, not just a color/size one. Reproduced with three plain `View`s at the exact scaled proportions (24→32px scale factor applied to each bar's measured x/y/width) — no `react-native-svg` dependency needed for three straight bars.

**3. Workspace/overall surface color corrected to web's actual measured value:** re-checked the live `getComputedStyle` proof from Sprint 40 (`body` background-color measured as `rgb(15, 23, 42)`) against `theme.ts`'s `workspace` token — `workspace` was `#212225` (reusing `backgroundElement`'s value, a Sprint 39 mobile-native design choice made before Stage 1's "measure web exactly" mandate existed), not `#0F172A`, web's actual measured value (which is `background`'s existing value in this same token set). Corrected `workspace` to `#0F172A` (dark) / `#ECEDF2` (light) — identical to `background`'s values now, kept as a separate token name in case semantic separation matters again later, but no longer a different color. This affects every screen using `Workspace` at once, not just the Header — the Header simply made the mismatch visible first.

**4. Friend-request icon + badge added to the Header, matching web's `.add`/`.addBadge`:** the icon (`addFriend.svg`/`addFriendDark.svg`) is a two-tone asset — `theme.text` fill plus a **fixed `#F17C7C` accent baked into the file itself**, confirmed by opening the raw SVG rather than assumed from the filename convention that held for every other icon checked so far. Badge matches Sprint 37's own measured values exactly (16px, `-6/-8` offset, `#e5484d`, 10px/700 text). Removed the Profile tab's badge (`app-tabs.tsx`) in the same change — web has exactly one location for this signal, and leaving both would show the same count twice.

**Asset-pipeline correction, worth recording precisely:** the friend-request icon was first attempted via `qlmanage`-based SVG-to-PNG rasterization (macOS's built-in QuickLook thumbnailer). It reported `hasAlpha: yes` but rendered as a solid white square in the app — QuickLook's thumbnail generator flattens transparency onto an opaque matte for preview purposes; the alpha channel exists structurally but isn't actually transparent. Caught before compounding into a bigger workaround (a Playwright-based rasterization script was about to be built next) by stepping back and checking the project's own existing asset pipeline first: `expo-image` already renders `.svg` files natively, and this codebase already has a working example (`login-form.tsx`'s `google-icon.svg`). Switched to copying `addFriend.svg`/`addFriendDark.svg` verbatim (checksum-verified) and referencing them directly — no rasterization, no new dependency, and the two-tone color detail (which a tint-based approach would have lost) is preserved exactly because it's still a real SVG. The broken PNGs were deleted, not left behind.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live after a full relaunch, both themes: logo navigation, the correctly-shaped hamburger glyph, the darker/corrected surface color, and the friend-request icon rendering with its actual two-tone color (not a flat tint) all confirmed. The Sprint 38 remount-race warning (already flagged as out of scope) reappeared during this round of testing too — same known issue, not new, not investigated further here.

## Stage 1: Header — rendered web UI supersedes source-code parity for three items

**Context:** the previous entry closed the Header out on source-code parity grounds. That was explicitly rejected — Stage 1 is about visual and behavioral parity, not source parity, and a rendered comparison was requested. Before implementing anything, three requirements were checked against `header.tsx`'s fresh source and found to directly contradict it: a back button in the shared Header (source has none there — `ScreenHeader` owns that), true logo centering (source uses `justify-content: space-between`, which only looks centered when both sides are equal width), and a fully-conditional friend-request icon (source always renders the icon, only the badge is conditional). Flagged before implementing, per this project's standing discipline. The response was explicit and unambiguous: stop treating web's source code as authoritative for this task — the rendered UI and the stated requirements are the specification going forward, superseding the source-driven reading.

**Implemented exactly as specified, superseding the prior entry's three points:**
1. **Back button added to the shared Header**, left-aligned — web's own `backWhite.svg`/`backDark.svg`, copied verbatim (checksum-verified), `router.back()` on press. No conflict with `ScreenHeader`: this `Header` only renders on the four tab roots, which never simultaneously show a pushed screen's own `ScreenHeader` — the two are mutually exclusive by route, not overlapping responsibilities.
2. **Brand block genuinely centered** via two equal-width `flex: 1` zones (back button in the left one, friend-request+menu in the right one) — not web's own `space-between`, which was confirmed to visibly shift the logo whenever the two sides' content widths differ. This is a deliberate departure from web's literal mechanism to satisfy an explicit "stays centered regardless" requirement.
3. **Friend-request icon and badge are now a single conditional unit** (`pendingRequests > 0`) — reverted from the prior entry's "always visible, badge-only conditional," which matched web's source exactly but was explicitly overridden.

**Still outstanding:** an actual screenshot of web's rendered, authenticated Header (at matching viewport width) was requested for final pixel-level validation but was not received in this conversation — implementation proceeded on the explicit written requirements alone, which were specific enough to act on. Header height, exact padding, and color-for-color comparison against the real rendered screenshot remain unverified until that image is provided.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean. Verified live after a full relaunch (dark theme, real data, zero pending requests): back button present and left-aligned, brand block visibly centered in the row, friend-request icon correctly absent (only the menu button remains on the right) — all three confirmed matching the explicit requirements. Not yet confirmed against an actual web screenshot.

**Immediately after:** the decision-history commentary in this entry's own doc comment inside `header.tsx` (the "superseded an earlier pass" narrative reproduced above) was stripped from the component source, per direct instruction that the source should document only the current implementation — the history stays here, not in code. No behavior change.

## Sprint 33 — AppShell rebuild: Header, Navigation and Content as first-class shell primitives

**Context:** Navigation was deliberately left for last across this whole Stage 1 arc, flagged repeatedly as "the largest architectural change." Auditing web's actual `AppShell.tsx` (rather than assuming the obvious "give the bottom tabs web's colors" reading) surfaced a mismatch far bigger than a restyle:

- Web's `AppShell` renders `Header`, `{session && <Navigation/>}`, then a bordered `.content` div **once**, at the app root, wrapping every authenticated page. `Header`/`Navigation` are DOM siblings of routed content, never nested inside it.
- Web's primary `Navigation` (`app/components/navigation/navigation.tsx`) is three **text-only** pills — Profile / Training / Users — not icons, and not four items. There is no Home or Explore entry in it at all: Home is reached solely via the header logo; Explore's content (the exercise "Wiki") and "Complexes" are reached through a second, narrower sub-nav (`trainingMenu.tsx`) that only appears inside Training, fixed to the bottom of the viewport, and stays visible across every one of Training's own child routes (list, detail, create).
- Mobile's shell was the inverse on both counts: `AppTabs` (`expo-router`'s `NativeTabs`) was a native bottom bar with four icon destinations (Home, Programs, Explore, Profile), and `Header` was rendered per-screen, nested *inside* `Workspace`'s own bordered content box — a state `docs/decisions.md` had already flagged (Sprint 39, "Known, tracked imperfection") as pending a deferred "Phase 1: Screen composition primitive" that never got built.

Per explicit direction this sprint, scope was widened twice beyond "restyle the tab bar": (1) build the proper Header/Navigation/content-as-siblings structure now rather than defer it again, and (2) make Header+Navigation genuinely global — every authenticated screen, not just the four former tab roots — matching web's actual `AppShell` exactly, even though that reopens a standing decision to keep pushed/detail screens header-free. A full written migration plan (current architecture, target architecture, components removed/introduced, routing/chrome ownership, risks, migration order) was produced and approved before any code changed.

**Decision — architecture:**
- New `src/components/app-shell.tsx`: owns the top safe-area inset once (`SafeAreaView edges={['top']}`), renders `Header` then `Navigation`, then whatever routed content follows as a sibling — not a child of either. Mounted once in `src/app/(app)/_layout.tsx`, wrapping a plain `<Stack screenOptions={{headerShown:false}}/>`, so it never remounts across navigation (verified: `Navigation`'s active-pill state updates reactively via `usePathname()` while the same `Header` instance stays mounted).
- New `src/components/navigation.tsx`: the Profile/Training/Users pill bar. Ported `navigation.module.scss`'s mobile-width rules (the only ones relevant — every RN viewport is "mobile-width" on this axis): `space-around` row, 2px bordered pills with only the top corners rounded, `minWidth:100`, `4px`/`12px` padding. Active state matches web's literal mechanism, not an approximation: the active pill drops its own bottom border (RN: `borderBottomColor:'transparent'`) so it visually opens into the content box directly beneath it — which shares the same border color — plus a real `View` underline under the label at 105% width (RN has no `:after`), reproducing web's `span:after`. `isActive` copied web's exact per-item logic (`pathname === '/profile'`, `pathname.startsWith('/programs')` for Training, `pathname === '/users'`) rather than a startsWith-everywhere simplification — confirmed live that this correctly leaves all three pills unhighlighted on pushed screens like Settings, matching web's own behavior there (web's Settings page isn't nested under `/profile` either, so its Profile pill doesn't light up).
- New `src/components/training-sub-nav.tsx`: the Complexes/Programs/Wiki pill bar, ported from `trainingMenu.module.scss`. Web's `position:fixed` became `position:'absolute'` anchored to `programs/_layout.tsx`'s own full-height wrapper (no portal needed — RN has no viewport-relative fixed positioning, but anchoring to a screen-height ancestor gives the same visual result). Web's active indicator is an elaborate CSS border-trick chevron under the label; reproduced instead with the same active language `Navigation` already uses (filled `backgroundSelected` + label color swap) — one consistent active-state vocabulary across both nav bars rather than porting a second, different mechanism. Rendered once in `programs/_layout.tsx`, wrapping its `<Stack/>`, so it's visible on every Training child route exactly like web's own `training/layout.tsx`.
- `Workspace` (`src/components/workspace.tsx`) already had an unused `topInset` escape hatch from the deferred Sprint 39 plan; every `(app)`-side call site now passes `topInset={false}`, since `AppShell` owns that edge globally. `Workspace`'s own bordered content box is otherwise untouched and reused as-is — no second border implementation.

**Decision — routing/file moves, reusing existing routing rather than introducing a new mechanism:**
- `src/components/app-tabs.tsx` (+ `.web.tsx`) deleted outright — web has no app-wide bottom bar to port.
- `explore/{index,[id]}.tsx` moved to `programs/wiki/{index,[id]}.tsx`; the standalone `explore/_layout.tsx` route group retired. Matches web's actual `/training/wiki` nesting and lets one `TrainingSubNav` cover Complexes/Programs/Wiki from a single layout, the same way web's one `training/layout.tsx` does.
- `profile/users.tsx` moved to `src/app/(app)/users/index.tsx`, promoted to a primary top-level destination (a new `Stack.Screen name="users"` alongside `index`/`programs`/`profile` in `(app)/_layout.tsx`) — Users is one of web's three primary nav pills, not a screen nested under Profile.
- `Stack.Protected` guards in the root `_layout.tsx` were untouched — already matches web's `{session && <Navigation/>}` gating structurally, since unauthenticated users never enter `(app)` at all.

**Decision — redundant chrome removed, not carried forward:** per explicit direction ("do not preserve old architecture just because it already exists"), every screen's own `<Header/>` (the four former tab roots) and every pushed screen's pure back-navigation `<ScreenHeader backHref=.../>` / `onBackPress={() => router.back()}` were removed — Header's own back button now covers every case globally, matching web (which has no per-page back link anywhere). Two `ScreenHeader` call sites were kept deliberately, not swept: `programs/create.tsx` and `programs/exercise-picker.tsx`'s "Cancel" actions do real work beyond navigation (`resetWizard()`, discarding an uncommitted local selection) — genuine page actions, not chrome. Where a removed `ScreenHeader` also carried a `title` (`requests.tsx`, `friends.tsx`, `complexes/index.tsx`), that text was kept as a plain heading matching web's own real `<h2>` on those pages (`requests.tsx` → "Requests"; `complexes/index.tsx` → **"Complexes"**, not the previous "Global Programs" — web's own heading, confirmed directly against `complexes.tsx`'s `<h2 className={'pageTitle'}>Complexes</h2>`, even though the fetch helpers are internally named for global programs). `users/index.tsx` got no heading at all, matching web's own heading-free `/users` page exactly.

Also removed as now-redundant, now that real entry points exist: `profile/index.tsx`'s "Friends"/"Requests"/"Users"/"Settings" plain-text link rows (Friends already has its own `FriendsPreview` "see all"; Requests has Header's friend-request icon; Users and Settings now have their own real nav entries) and `programs/index.tsx`'s "Browse Global Programs →" link (explicitly flagged as a temporary Stage 2 item in the Global Programs entry, conditioned on "until Navigation is addressed" — it now is, via `TrainingSubNav`).

**Decision — `BottomTabInset` narrowed to where it's still true:** this constant was sized to clear the old native tab bar's own height. With that bar gone, it was removed from every non-Training screen's `contentStyle` (Home, Profile, Users, Settings, Friends, Requests, `[id]`) and kept only on Training-family screens, where it now clears `TrainingSubNav` instead — a coincidentally-adequate existing value, not a newly-tuned one.

**Verified false alarm, worth recording:** mid-walkthrough, `TrainingSubNav` appeared to show two pills simultaneously "active" (Complexes and Programs both looked filled while on the Complexes screen). Pixel-sampled the actual screenshot rather than trusting the visual read: `Programs`' background was confirmed `#0F172A` (`workspace`, correctly inactive) at every point away from its own text glyph — what looked like a fill was the inactive label's own light `theme.title` color (`#f3e8dd`), sampled by eye near the pill's center where the text sits. No logic bug existed; `usePathname()` and both `isActive` predicates were confirmed correct via a temporary on-screen debug print before being ruled out and removed.

**Still outstanding, unchanged from the prior Header entry:** an actual screenshot of web's rendered UI (Header or the new Navigation/TrainingSubNav) was never provided in this conversation. Implementation and validation proceeded on web's real source (CSS/TSX, measured directly, not from memory) plus the written requirements — not a pixel-level comparison against a real render.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` clean after every step, not just at the end. Verified live after multiple full relaunches: Home (Header+Navigation, no pill active, no duplicate chrome), Profile (Profile pill active, redundant links gone, settings gear + Programs link intact), Training → Programs/Complexes/Wiki (Training pill active throughout, correct sub-nav highlighting, no duplicate "Browse Global Programs" link), Users (Users pill active, no heading), Settings reached via the Header menu (global chrome present, no pill active — matches web), Program Detail pushed from a list (Training pill still active, sub-nav still visible, single back button, no leftover manual back-link), and back-navigation returning to the correct prior screen in every case.

## Sprint 34 — Floating glass shell (icon Navigation, Header redesign) + chrome-clearance fix

**Context:** this entry is written retroactively, covering a large block of shell work that landed across several sessions without an interim doc pass, plus a same-session fix. The summary below (everything up to "Chrome clearance" ) is reconstructed from the diff itself rather than from an in-conversation decision log — accurate on *what* changed, not a full blow-by-blow of *why* each call was made the way Sprint 33's entry above is.

**What changed since Sprint 33 — `Header`/`Navigation` became floating glass bars, not docked chrome:**
- `AppShell` (`src/components/app-shell.tsx`) no longer owns a top `SafeAreaView` or lays `Header`/`Navigation` into a bordered flow row. Both are now self-positioning (`position: absolute`, sized off `useSafeAreaInsets()` directly) and rendered as siblings of a full-bleed `{children}` — routed content is genuinely full-height and can scroll behind both bars. `{children}` is wrapped in `expo-blur`'s `BlurTargetView`, passed to both bars as `blurTarget`, since Android's `BlurView` only renders a real blur (not a flat tint) when told which view's content to sample.
- `Navigation` (`src/components/navigation.tsx`) replaced web's three text pills (Profile/Training/Users) with an Instagram/Threads-style floating icon bar — five items (Home/Friends/Training/Messages/Profile), animated active-pill background + icon scale via `react-native-reanimated`, a friend-request count badge on the Friends icon (moved off the old Profile-tab badge). A deliberate, explicit mobile-native divergence from web's own nav shape, not a parity port.
- `Header` (`src/components/header.tsx`) gained a left-aligned back button, a centered logo+"COREFIT" wordmark, and a right-side hamburger menu (Settings + theme toggle) replacing the old friend-request icon there (which now lives on the Navigation badge instead). New brand assets (`assets/images/brand/logo-{dark,light}.png`).
- New `theme.glassBorder` token (light/dark) — a soft hairline for the glass edge, deliberately with no accompanying background fill (blur alone separates content from chrome, not a color overlay).
- `BottomTabInset` zeroed out (`src/constants/theme.ts`) — the floating `Navigation`'s own clearance (`getFloatingNavClearance`) replaced it globally; kept as a named constant at its ~8 existing call sites rather than removed, to avoid an unrelated diff through Training's screens.
- New placeholder `messages.tsx` screen and `Stack.Screen name="messages"` — the nav item exists ahead of the feature so the bar's shape doesn't change again once messaging ships.
- The four `(auth)` screens plus `auth-callback.tsx`/`reset-password.tsx` migrated from `ScreenLayout` onto `Workspace` — a mechanical consolidation onto the one shell component, no behavior change.

**Chrome clearance — bug found and fixed this session, in code inherited from the above:** `Home`, `profile/friends.tsx`, and `profile/requests.tsx` each had a top-clearance bug: the always-rendered heading (or, on Home, the loading skeleton/error block) rendered flush against the very top of the screen, overlapping the floating Header, while the `FlatList` beside it was correctly offset — because only the `FlatList`'s own `contentContainerStyle` carried `clearance.top`, not its sibling. First fix attempt moved `clearance.top` onto the shared `Workspace` container's `paddingTop` instead — this visibly corrected the heading, but silently broke Home's scroll-behind-header effect: padding an ancestor shrinks a scrolling descendant's own frame in RN, so `FlatList`'s viewport itself started below the header instead of spanning full-height with the header floating over it, exactly the regression `workspace.tsx`'s own doc comment already warns about. Caught by the user scrolling and noticing content stopped reaching the top edge instead of passing behind the glass. Corrected by giving clearance to whichever sibling is actually topmost instead of the shared container: `FlatList`'s own `contentContainerStyle.paddingTop` restored (Home, where it's the sole top-level child in its state), and a plain `marginTop: clearance.top` added to the one always-first heading on Friends/Requests (where `FlatList` is never the topmost sibling, so it needs no clearance of its own). Verified live on-emulator for all three screens, including confirming Home's list still scrolls behind the header after the correction.

**Header/Navigation glass tuned more transparent:** both `BlurView`s' `intensity` lowered from 85 to 45 — the prior value read as near-opaque; 45 keeps the frost/separation but lets scrolled content read clearly through it. A margin/inset reduction (16px → 8px) was discussed and explicitly declined, since `HEADER_MARGIN`/`NAV_MARGIN` also drive the bars' horizontal insets and shrinking it would dock the bars closer to the screen edges, undercutting the floating-pill look.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (one pre-existing, unrelated lint error in `use-color-scheme.web.ts` left as-is). Verified live on an Android emulator across multiple reloads: Home skeleton/error/list all correctly clear the header, list content scrolls behind the glass header again, Friends/Requests headings sit just below the header with no oversized gap to the list beneath.

## Sprint 35 — Training sub-nav rebuilt as a floating glass bar

**Context:** `TrainingSubNav` (Complexes/Programs/Wiki) was the one piece of chrome Sprint 34 didn't touch — still docked in normal flow directly below the Header, opaque, via a `programs/_layout.tsx` wrapper that padded its own `View` by `clearance.top`. That's the exact ancestor-padding anti-pattern already found and fixed for Home/Friends/Requests: it shrinks every scrolling descendant's own frame, so Training content couldn't reach the top or bottom edges the way Home's already does. Explicit direction this round: rebuild it as a third floating glass bar, stacked above the main `Navigation`, with Training content scrolling behind all three (Header, sub-nav, Navigation) same as Home.

**Decision — floating bar, reusing Header/Navigation's exact mechanism:** `TrainingSubNav` now self-positions (`position: absolute`, sized off `useSafeAreaInsets()`), `BlurView` glass treatment, own exported `getFloatingSubNavClearance()`. `programs/_layout.tsx` no longer pads a wrapper `View` — `<Stack>` and `<TrainingSubNav/>` are plain siblings, mirroring `AppShell` itself. New `useTrainingChromeClearance()` (`use-chrome-clearance.ts`) gives Training screens a `bottom` that additionally clears the sub-nav; `Workspace` gained an optional `bottomClearance` prop so its `scroll` branch's internally-owned `ScrollView` can take this training-aware value instead of the plain one. All 8 screens under `(app)/programs/` updated to the same rule already established for Home/Friends/Requests — clearance goes on whichever sibling is actually topmost or actually scrolling (a `marginTop` on a heading/`ScreenHeader`, or a `paddingTop`/`paddingBottom` on the one real `FlatList`/`ScrollView`), never on a shared ancestor. `ProgramsList` and `ScreenHeader` each gained a small style-passthrough prop to make this possible without new one-off components.

**Bug found and fixed — native SIGSEGV from a self-referential BlurView:** first attempt threaded Header/Navigation's own `blurTarget` ref down to `TrainingSubNav` via a new React context, since it renders deep inside routed content rather than as a direct `AppShell` sibling. Crashed immediately on opening Training — `Fatal signal 11 (SIGSEGV) ... RenderThread`, confirmed via `adb logcat`'s crash tombstone. Root cause: `TrainingSubNav` was a *descendant* of the very `BlurTargetView` it was told to sample, a self-referential snapshot Android's compositor can't handle. Fixed properly (not just avoided) by giving `programs/_layout.tsx` its own local `BlurTargetView` wrapping only `<Stack>` — `TrainingSubNav` samples that one instead, as a sibling, never a descendant. Restores real blur-through (not just a flat fallback tint), so the sub-nav's glass now renders identically to Header/Navigation rather than an approximation.

**Bug found and fixed — Android elevation/borderRadius clipping seam:** the bar's outer `View` carried `shadowColor`/`elevation` *and* `borderRadius`/`overflow:'hidden'` together — Android casts an `elevation` shadow off the view's full rectangular bounds before the radius/clip is applied, so a sliver of the unclipped rectangle showed past the rounded corners as a pale seam along the bottom edge. Split across two nodes (outer carries shadow/elevation only, inner carries radius/clip only) — the standard fix, and worth checking for on Header/Navigation's own identical `styles.bar` pattern if it ever surfaces there too.

**Follow-up round, same session — three explicit refinements after seeing it live:**
1. **Muscle-group filter moved from a vertical rail to a horizontal one** (`MuscleGroupFilter`, `wiki/index.tsx`) — the vertical rail's lower tabs (Legs, Abs) rendered underneath the new floating sub-nav with no way to scroll them into view, and taps there hit the sub-nav's own `BlurView` instead (it fills its full rect regardless of visual content). A horizontal, independently-scrolling row above the list sidesteps the problem entirely rather than just padding around it — an explicit mobile-native divergence from web's vertical sidebar, chosen by the user from three options (fix-in-place / horizontal-top / vertical-right).
2. **Sub-nav's active tab gained a real border** (`theme.border`, 2px, rounded) rather than only a fill, and the gap up to the main `Navigation` bar was tightened — a new `getFloatingNavTopEdge()` export on `navigation.tsx` (the bar's top edge with none of `getFloatingNavClearance`'s extra margin) plus a smaller dedicated `NAV_GAP` constant, so the two bars read as a tighter, more visually-connected stack without touching `NAV_MARGIN` itself (still doing double duty for Navigation's own edge insets).
3. **`Wiki`'s title had redundant double spacing** — its own `marginBottom` stacked with `Workspace`'s `contentStyle.gap`, both pushing the same seam. Removed the redundant `marginBottom`.

**Inputs on light theme barely readable, fixed separately:** `ExerciseSearchBar` and `WorkoutLogForm`'s `TextInput`s used only a `theme.backgroundElement` fill with no border — nearly indistinguishable from `theme.workspace` in light mode (`#F0F0F3` vs `#ECEDF2`). `AuthTextField` already had the answer in the same codebase (`borderWidth: 2, borderColor: theme.border`, matching the app's established bordered-not-filled language) — applied the same treatment to both, rather than inventing a second convention.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean throughout (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator after every step, including a deliberately careful re-check after re-introducing `blurTarget`: Programs/Complexes/Wiki all show the sub-nav floating above Navigation with a visible, tightened gap, both bars rendering identical glass; content scrolls behind Header, sub-nav, and Navigation on every screen (list and detail); the horizontal muscle-group filter's previously-unreachable tabs (confirmed via screenshot: Legs, Abs) are now reachable and functional; no crashes across ~15 full relaunches during this round.

## Sprint 36 — Program Detail rebuilt as a direct visual port of web's TrainingProcessing

**Context:** this entry picks up code already written in a prior session but left uncommitted — found in that state at session start (`git status` showed `[id].tsx`/`workout-log-form.tsx`/`programs.ts` modified and `workout-history.tsx` deleted, nothing staged). Verified rather than re-derived: read the full diff, confirmed `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` were already clean, then live-tested on an Android emulator before treating it as done.

**What the carried-over code does:** Program Detail (`programs/[id].tsx`) gained a title row with inline edit/delete icon buttons (replacing the old text links), an `author` field joined from `profiles` via a new `author_id` embed in `getProgramDetail` (`lib/supabase/programs.ts`), and a web-mirroring I/II/III view-density switch (thumbnail / single truncated line / wrapping paragraph — cosmetic only, default II unchanged from prior behavior). `workout-history.tsx` was deleted; `WorkoutLogForm` absorbed its job and was rewritten from a single-column exercise list into web's actual three-column `TrainingProcessing` layout — a fixed name column, a horizontally-scrollable per-date history column, and a fixed input column, row-height-synced across all three since RN (unlike CSS) can't share one height rule across three sibling scrolling trees.

**Bug found and fixed this session — density 3's wrapping text overflowed its fixed row height:** every density used one `rowHeight(viewDensity)` constant, including density 3 ("wrapping paragraph, no truncation"). A 3-line exercise name (e.g. "Incline Bench Press") overflowed the 56px constant meant for ~2 lines, visually overlapping the next row and desyncing the name column from the history/input columns beside it for every row after — caught live on an Android emulator (Rak 6-d, Day 1), not from reading the diff. Fixed by making density 3's row height content-derived instead of constant: each name `Text` reports its actual wrapped line count via `onTextLayout` into a `nameLineCounts` state map keyed by `programExerciseId`, and `density3RowHeight()` sizes that row from it (`max(56, lines × 20 + 16)`), applied consistently to that same exercise's name/history/input cells. Densities 1 and 2 are unaffected — their height is content-independent (fixed thumbnail size; single truncated line).

**Bug found and fixed — input placeholders vertically clipped:** flagged by the user from a screenshot after the above fix, not caught during the emulator pass. `WorkoutLogForm`'s `TextInput`s (`YYYY-MM-DD`, `XXX/YYxZ`) had no explicit `height`, no `paddingVertical`, and their parent cell was only 32px tall (density 2) — Android's `TextInput` intrinsic font padding pushed the glyphs taller than that, so the cell clipped their tops instead of centering them. Fixed with `height: '100%'` (fills the fixed-height parent cell instead of sizing off intrinsic content), `paddingVertical: 0`, `textAlignVertical: 'center'`, and `includeFontPadding: false` (the last two Android-only, no-ops on iOS) — the standard fix for this class of RN/Android `TextInput` clipping.

**Bug found and fixed — I/II/III density-tab border broken:** flagged by the user from a screenshot. The tab group used a segmented-control "collapsed border" trick — each `Pressable` had its own `borderWidth: 1` plus `marginLeft: -1` to overlap adjacent borders into one hairline — but the *outer* container had no border of its own, only `borderRadius`/`overflow: 'hidden'`. Two visible symptoms: the first tab's `marginLeft: -1` pushed its left border 1px outside the container's own left edge, where `overflow: 'hidden'` clipped it away entirely (left edge reads as borderless); and since each tab's border is a plain rectangle, clipping it against the container's *rounded* corners cut the stroke at a hard right angle instead of following the radius, reading as "the corners are half-missing." Fixed by moving the border onto the container itself (a real stroked-and-rounded border, not a clip-rounded one) and replacing the per-tab overlapping-border hack with plain `borderLeftWidth` dividers on every tab but the first.

**Feature added — density-2 tap-to-expand truncated exercise names, mirroring web's `li:has(span):hover`:** on web, tapping a truncated name on mobile browsers triggers CSS `:hover` (sticky until something else is tapped), which unclips the `span` and gives it a background/shadow so it visually floats over neighboring rows without shifting layout. RN has no `:hover`, so this is explicit tap-to-expand state (`expandedExerciseId`) instead — tapping a name shows it in full; tapping anywhere else in the day's grid (a `Pressable` wrapping the whole row, `onPress={() => setExpandedExerciseId(null)}`) collapses it, relying on RN's touch-responder system (a nested `Pressable`'s own `onPress` firing means the outer one doesn't) rather than manual propagation logic.

**Three compounding bugs fought through to get the overlay actually unclipped and unoccluded on Android** — each looked plausible and got proven wrong in turn, at real cost in back-and-forth:
1. **Intrinsic sizing:** the natural approach — `position: 'absolute'` with only `top`/`left` set, expecting Yoga to size the node to its own (now-unbounded) text content — instead stretched it to the *parent Pressable's* narrow, truncated-content width (Yoga's cross-axis `alignItems: 'stretch'` default still applies to absolutely-positioned children). Worked around by measuring the natural width out-of-band: an invisible copy of the same text, rendered off-screen but critically as a sibling of the *whole row* (`container`) rather than nested inside the narrow name column — nesting it there reproduced the exact same width-collapse on the "unconstrained" measurer itself, since an absolutely-positioned node's available layout width still comes from its nearest flex-constrained ancestor regardless of `left`/`top`. The measured pixel width, plus its own padding (since RN's `width` is a border-box size), is then applied to the real overlay directly.
2. **Android view clipping:** with an explicit, correctly-measured width, the overlay was *still* cut off at the exact same point — because Android's native `ViewGroup` clips children to its own bounds by default, and RN's `overflow: 'visible'` (already RN's own default) doesn't reach that native flag without being set explicitly on every ancestor in the chain. Diagnosed by hardcoding a large `width` and an obvious `red`/`blue` debug style — when even that came back identically clipped, `overflow: 'visible'` was added explicitly down the whole ancestor chain (`day`, `cell`, `nameColumn`, `nameCellRow`, `nameTruncatedWrap`).
3. **Sibling paint order:** overflow fixed, but the debug-red box was still only *partially* visible — a sliver peeking through in the gap between the history and input columns. Root cause: `zIndex` in RN only orders siblings under the *same* parent, and the overlay's parent chain put it several levels below the row's own history/input columns (siblings of the *name column*, not of the overlay) — so those later-painted siblings covered it regardless of its `zIndex`. Fixed by moving the overlay out of the name column entirely, rendering it as a sibling of the name/history/input columns themselves, last in JSX so it paints on top of all three — which in turn meant it needed its own Y position, since it's no longer a descendant of the row it's expanding (`nameRowTops`, each row's own `onLayout`-measured offset within the shared row container).

**Fixed — date/value `TextInput`s stretched to match density 1/3's taller rows:** flagged by the user from two screenshots (density 1 vs. density 2), asking that inputs always stay density 2's compact size, centered in whatever row height the density actually needs. `input`'s `height: '100%'` was filling the *row's* height (52px for density 1's thumbnail, however tall density 3's wrapped name needed) rather than owning a size of its own. Fixed with a fixed `INPUT_HEIGHT` (32, matching density 2's row) in place of `'100%'` — the parent `cell`'s existing `justifyContent: 'center'` (already there for exactly this purpose) now centers the fixed-size input within the taller row automatically, no other change needed.

## Sprint 37 — Program Detail's I/II/III view density persisted per-user

**Context:** explicit user request — the density tab (Sprint 36) reset to its default every time the screen remounted; wanted it remembered per-user, defaulting to `2` until changed at least once. Web's own `ProgramTabs` doesn't persist `activeTab` at all (always resets to 2 on mount), so this is a new mobile-only enhancement, not a parity port — flagged to the user as a decision point (device-local storage vs. syncing across the user's devices) before implementing; they picked syncing, matching the one existing precedent in this codebase for a per-user UI preference.

**Decision — mirror `themePreference`'s existing `profiles`-column pattern exactly, not a new mechanism:** this codebase has no AsyncStorage/MMKV/`expo-secure-store` usage anywhere and no Zustand `persist` middleware — the *only* established per-user preference (`profiles.dark`, the theme toggle) is a plain Supabase column, fetched into `auth-store.ts`'s Zustand store on every session change and written straight through `updateProfileById` on change. Reusing that shape instead of introducing a second, different persistence mechanism for a very similar feature: added a nullable `program_view_density smallint` column to `public.profiles` (via `supabase db query --linked`, the same way `dark`/`language`/etc. were evidently added — this project has no tracked migration files, so an ad hoc `ALTER TABLE` matches existing practice rather than introducing a migrations workflow that doesn't otherwise exist here).

**Implementation:** `profile.ts`'s `profileSchema`/`ProfileUpdate`/`PROFILE_COLUMNS` gained `program_view_density: z.union([z.literal(1), z.literal(2), z.literal(3)]).nullable()`, following `dark`'s exact shape. `auth-store.ts`'s single profile-refresh fetch (renamed `refreshThemePreference` → `refreshProfilePreferences`, since it now populates two preferences from one row rather than one) sets both `themePreference` and the new `viewDensity` field; `viewDensity` resets to `null` on sign-out same as `themePreference`. `programs/[id].tsx` replaced its local `useState<ViewDensity>(2)` with `useAuthStore((s) => s.viewDensity) ?? 2`, and `handleDensitySelect` mirrors Settings' own `handleThemeSelect`: instant-apply (no separate Save step, matching web's own theme toggle precedent), optimistic (`setStoredViewDensity` fires immediately so the tab switches with no delay; a failed `updateProfileById` write surfaces a small inline error rather than blocking or reverting the UI, since a preference silently not persisting is low-stakes compared to blocking the screen).

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator and directly against the linked Supabase project: selected density III on Rak 6-d, force-quit and relaunched the app — density III was still selected on return; switched back to II, force-quit/relaunched again — persisted correctly; `supabase db query --linked` against `public.profiles` confirmed `program_view_density = 2` on the test account's row, scoped to that one user (not a device-wide value).

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean throughout (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator: Program Detail renders title row with working edit/delete icons, author line, and all three density tabs against a real program (Rak 6-d) with multi-date history; re-checked density 3 specifically after the row-height fix — every row now aligns across all three columns regardless of wrapped line count; re-checked the input placeholders after the clipping fix — both `YYYY-MM-DD` and `XXX/YYxZ` render fully and vertically centered; re-checked the density-tab group after the border fix — full border on all four sides with a clean rounded rect, crisp single-pixel dividers between I/II/III; verified tap-to-expand after all three overlay fixes — tapping "1. Barbell Bench Press" (density 2, truncated to "1. Barbe…") now shows the full name floating cleanly over the row with no clipping and no occlusion by the history/input columns, and tapping elsewhere in the grid collapses it back to truncated; re-checked densities 1 and 3 after the fixed-input-height fix — every date/value input stays the same compact size and sits centered in the taller thumbnail/wrapped-name rows instead of stretching to fill them.

## Sprint 38 — `Workspace`'s bordered content panel removed, matching web

**Context:** while porting Header/Navigation to Web Mobile (a parallel, larger effort on the web repo — see that project's own history), web's `.content` (`app.module.scss`) turned out to still carry a `border: 2px solid`/`border-radius`/compensating negative margin at the new `<768px` floating-glass breakpoint — a desktop boxed-panel leftover that read as boxed-in now that Header/Navigation float over full-bleed content instead of docking above an in-flow panel. Removed there. `Workspace`'s own `content` style (`workspace.tsx`) carried the identical `borderWidth: 2, borderRadius: Spacing.one` — not a coincidence: its own comment says it was deliberately measured *from* web's border during an earlier parity sprint. Flagged by the user directly ("did you do the same on the RN project?") once the web fix was in; removing it here too was the obvious next step rather than leaving the two platforms newly inconsistent.

**Change:** `content`'s `borderWidth`/`borderRadius` removed from `workspace.tsx`'s `StyleSheet.create`, along with the now-dead `borderColor: theme.border` inline override at both of `Workspace`'s two call sites (`scroll` and non-`scroll` branches) — `padding`/`gap` are unrelated to the border and untouched.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator: Home's outer content frame is gone (full-bleed, edge-to-edge behind the floating Header/Navigation); individual `historyCard` borders (a separate, unrelated style) are still present and unaffected.

Requested for both this app and the web project in the same message; the web side is recorded in that project's own history.

**Decision — reuse the existing Profile pattern exactly for Settings, a lighter touch for the dropdown:** `profile/index.tsx`'s Sign out button (red `theme.danger` fill, `ThemedText type="smallBold"`, fires `signOut()` instantly with no confirmation dialog) is this app's one established precedent — copied verbatim onto `profile/settings.tsx`, at the very end of the screen, rather than inventing a second style for the same action. The Header dropdown is a small compact popup with two existing plain-text rows (Settings, Theme) — a full red button there would outweigh its siblings, so that one gets a plain row too, just with `themeColor="danger"` text as a hint, consistent with each surface's own existing weight rather than forcing one design across both.

**Implementation:** `header.tsx` — a third `Pressable` in `panelContent` calling `useAuthStore`'s `signOut` (already existed, just not called from here) after `closeMenu()`; `MENU_PANEL_HEIGHT` grown 107 → 151 (the fixed animated height the dropdown reveals to — needed room for the new row or it would've been clipped). `profile/settings.tsx` — the Profile screen's button copied as-is (style object included), placed after the Theme section.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator, logged in: opened the Header dropdown — all three rows (Settings, Theme, Sign out) render fully within the grown panel, nothing clipped; navigated to Settings — the red Sign out button renders at the very end, below Theme, matching Profile's existing button pixel-for-pixel.

That left Sign out reachable from three places at once; Profile's own copy was the original, but now redundant now that Header and Settings both cover the action.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator, logged in: Profile no longer shows a Sign out button; sign-out still works via the Header dropdown and Settings.

## Sprint 40 — App language: device auto-detect on first launch, manual override persists (infra only, Phase 0+1)

**Context:** explicit user request — on first launch, if the user has never manually picked a language, the app should mirror the phone's system language (falling back to English if unsupported); a manual pick in Settings must always be remembered and never get silently overridden by auto-detection again, even if the phone's language later changes. No i18n system existed at all before this — every UI string was hardcoded English. Full translation of the ~35 files carrying UI copy is out of scope here and deferred to a later sprint; this one is infrastructure only, covering the detection/persistence mechanism itself plus a minimal, working end-to-end slice (the Settings language picker) to prove it.

**Stack:** `i18next` + `react-i18next` + `expo-localization` (added via `npx expo install`, `expo-localization` plugin registered in `app.config.ts`). Local persistence reuses `expo-secure-store` (already a dependency, already this app's one precedent for device-local storage in `src/lib/supabase/storage.ts`) rather than adding `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` for two short strings.

**New module — `src/lib/i18n/`:**
- `language-storage.ts` — the same web/native split as `authStorage`, with two keys: `corefit-language` (the active `'en'|'uk'|'ru'`) and `corefit-language-is-manual` (set once a human, not auto-detection, has chosen a language — on this device or synced in from another one).
- `locales/{en,uk,ru}.json` — seeded with only `profile.settings.language` for now; the rest of the app's copy stays hardcoded English until the next sprint.
- `index.ts` — `initI18n()` (idempotent): reads the stored language first, and if one exists, applies it and stops there — `expo-localization` is never even consulted once anything is stored, which is what makes device-locale detection a true first-launch-only event. Only on a genuine first launch (nothing stored) does it call `Localization.getLocales()`, match against `['en','uk','ru']`, and fall back to `'en'` on no match — then persist that result immediately so every later launch takes the "already stored" branch. Also exports `setLanguagePreference()` (manual pick: applies instantly via `i18n.changeLanguage`, persists locally as manual, best-effort mirrors to `profiles.language`) and `reconcileLanguageWithProfile()` (see below).

**Where it hooks in:** `src/app/_layout.tsx`'s `RootNavigator` now awaits `initI18n()` alongside its existing `status === 'idle'/'loading'` gate, rendering nothing (same invisible-behind-`AnimatedSplashOverlay` technique already used for session restore) until the language is resolved — unlike `themePreference`, this can't be backfilled after first paint since text must render already-translated. No `I18nextProvider` needed; `react-i18next`'s `useTranslation()` binds to the global instance on its own.

**Cross-device sync:** language is deliberately *not* added to `useAuthStore` — it has to work pre-login (the `(auth)` stack renders before any session exists), so local storage is its sole runtime source of truth, independent of auth state. What auth-store.ts's existing `refreshProfilePreferences` (the same fire-and-forget fetch that already loads `themePreference`/`viewDensity` off the same `profiles` row) gains is one more call, `reconcileLanguageWithProfile(userId, profile.language)`: a manual local pick wins and is mirrored up to the profile; absent that, a manual pick found on the profile (made on a different device) is adopted here too, and is itself marked manual locally so this device is likewise protected from future auto-detection.

**Settings UI:** `profile/settings.tsx` gained a "Language" section, copying the existing `THEME_OPTIONS` pill-row pattern verbatim (`styles.section`/`optionRow`/`optionPill`, same selected/unselected background) — three pills, English/Українська/Русский, calling `setLanguagePreference`.

**Bugs found and fixed during live verification (not part of the original plan):** adding the Language section made Settings' content taller than the screen, and `Workspace` there was never in `scroll` mode (it fit without one before) — Sign out became unreachable, clipped behind the floating Navigation bar with no way to scroll to it. Fixed by adding `scroll`/`bottomClearance` to Settings' `Workspace` call, matching the pattern already used by other long-content screens (`programs/[id].tsx`, `programs/wiki/[id].tsx`); `contentStyle`'s padding was adjusted from manually including `clearance.top`/`clearance.bottom` to just the extra `Spacing.four` breathing room, since the `scroll` branch already applies both clearances itself. Flagged separately, explicitly out of scope, and left untouched: page titles are inconsistently sized app-wide (`type="title"` at 48px on Settings/Create program vs. `subtitle` at 32px on Messages vs. a custom style on Programs) — pre-existing, unrelated to this sprint, deferred to its own future task.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Verified live on an Android emulator via `cmd locale set-app-locales`/`pm clear` cycles: a cleared install with the device set to Ukrainian resolves to `uk` on first launch; the same with an unsupported locale (French) falls back to `en`; once anything is stored, changing the device's language and relaunching no longer changes the app's language — auto-detection genuinely only fires once. In Settings, tapping each language pill switches the "Language" section's own label/pills instantly (confirmed via screenshot diff before/after tap) — the rest of the UI stays English by design, since string translation is Phase 2. After the scroll fix, Settings scrolls correctly and Sign out is fully reachable.

## Sprint 41 — Phase 2: full UI translation sweep + Polish support (mobile), matching CoreFit web

**Context:** direct follow-on to Sprint 40 (i18n infrastructure only — every screen's own copy was still hardcoded English at that point). Explicit user request: translate the entire app, and add Polish as a 4th language — "здається вона в мене і була" (it seems I already had it), which turned out to be true only on the sister web project (`CoreFit`), not here. Requested for both projects in the same message; the web-side fixes are recorded further down in this same entry, since both landed in one combined session rather than as separate sprints.

**Research before implementation:** confirmed `CoreFit`'s `lib/languages.ts` already ships complete, human-translated `eng`/`rus`/`ukr`/`pl` vocabulary (`base`/`settings`/`training` namespaces) — reused verbatim wherever mobile's own English string matched or closely corresponded (Settings/Theme labels, auth field labels, program type/difficulty/muscle-group names, `cancelRequest`/`removeFriend`/`seeAllFriends`/etc.), rather than re-translating from scratch, per an explicit consistency policy agreed with the user. A handful of clear typos in web's existing translations were corrected while borrowing them (`Комлпексы`→`Комплексы`, `Реестрация`→`Регистрация`, `Призвіще`→`Прізвище`) rather than propagated. Strings with no web equivalent (Friends/Requests screens, exercise picker, Complexes/Wiki-specific UI, all thrown-`Error` messages) were translated fresh, in the same register as their nearest reused neighbors.

**Scope:** every hardcoded string across ~35 files — all `(auth)` screens, all shared components (`login-form`/`register-form`/`header`/`training-sub-nav`/`friends-preview`/`exercise-search-bar`/`muscle-group-filter`/`program-card`/`user-card`/`workout-log-form`), `(app)` root screens, all of `profile/*`, all of `programs/*` (create/detail/picker/complexes/wiki), and `users/index.tsx`. Landed as 9 sequential batches (one commit each — `i18n: add Polish support, consolidate enum formatters` through `i18n: translate users screen`), each independently `tsc`/`expo lint`/prettier-clean and key-parity-checked before moving to the next, rather than one enormous commit.

**Batch 0 — infrastructure + consolidation (done first, everything else depends on it):**
- `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` → `['en', 'uk', 'ru', 'pl']`; new `pl.json`, `resources` in `i18n.init()` gets a 4th entry.
- New `src/lib/format-enums.ts` consolidates `formatProgramType`/`formatProgramLevel` (previously `lib/supabase/programs.ts` — the wrong layer for UI strings) and `wiki/[id].tsx`'s near-duplicate local `formatExerciseType` into one translated module, all three now taking `t`.
- **Bug found and fixed**: `lib/supabase/exercises.ts`'s `localizeExercise()` locale-resolution ternary silently fell through to `name_ru` for any locale other than `'en'`/`'uk'` — not the documented `name_en` fallback. Latent until Polish became selectable; would have shown Polish users Russian exercise names. Fixed with an explicit per-locale lookup object instead of a ternary chain.
- Shared `nameSchema` (`lib/validation.ts`) → `createNameSchema(t)` factory, consumed via `useMemo` — zod messages need `t`, only available inside a component. Same factory-fn treatment later applied to every other module-scope zod schema in the app (`login-form`/`register-form`/`forgot-password`/`reset-password`/`programs/create`'s `nameSchema` and its `confirmRemoval` dialog builder).
- Thrown `Error`s in `supabase/auth.ts`/`supabase/friends.ts` now carry stable keys (`google_oauth_no_url` etc.) instead of English sentences, translated only at the UI catch-site via `t(\`errors.${key}\`, { defaultValue: key })`.
- Deleted 3 confirmed-unused Expo-template leftovers: `hint-row.tsx`, `ui/collapsible.tsx`, `web-badge.tsx`.

**Batches 1–6 — the actual sweep**, in dependency order (auth → shared components → app roots → profile → programs → users), each reusing already-shipped keys from earlier batches wherever the exact same string recurred (`common.retry`, `common.cancel`/`back`/`next`/`save`/`delete`, `programs.day` template, `programs.loadingProgram`, etc.) rather than duplicating per-screen. Three worked examples of trickier cases, all following patterns already established in Sprint 40:
- `confirm-email.tsx`'s conditional string-concatenation JSX (`` `to${email ? ... : ...}` ``) collapsed into one `{{email}}`-interpolated key, resolved *before* `t()` rather than inside JSX — the old shape assumed English word order.
- `programs/create.tsx`'s day/exercise-removal confirm dialog counts two independently-pluralized values in one sentence; i18next can't express that in a single key, so the existing "build parts, join" structure stays, but every part (including the joiner word, `common.and`) is now translated separately.
- `profile/friends.tsx`'s `{{name}}'s Friends` title/empty-state: the English possessive shape stays English-only — uk/ru/pl rephrase as "friends of {{name}}" instead, since mirroring English word order there reads wrong.
- Every pluralized key (day/exercise counts) carries the full `_one`/`_few`/`_many`/`_other` CLDR set for uk/ru/pl (English only needs `_one`/`_other`) — Polish's forms were written from its own boundary rules, not copied from Russian/Ukrainian despite the shared Slavic root, per the plan's explicit warning against that mistake.

**Verification tooling — new `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs`:** flattens all 4 locale JSONs, treats plural-suffixed keys (`_one`/`_few`/`_many`/`_other`/`_zero`) as one logical key for base-key parity (so `en` legitimately having fewer plural forms than `uk`/`ru`/`pl` isn't flagged), then separately asserts uk/ru/pl each carry the full 4-form set for every key pluralized in any locale. Exits non-zero on any gap; kept in `scripts/` (already home to `reset-project.js`) as a reusable check, not a one-off. Passed clean at 192 base keys across all 4 locales as of the final batch.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error), and the parity script all clean after every batch, not just at the end. Live on an Android emulator: English renders correctly (pre-existing baseline, unchanged); a cleared install with the device locale set to Ukrainian correctly auto-detects and renders the entire `(auth)` stack in Ukrainian pre-login (title, field labels/placeholders, buttons, links — every Batch 1/2 key) — the single hardest case, since it must work with no session and no manual pick. Polish and Russian live rendering could not be re-confirmed in the same session: the emulator degraded after this sprint's many consecutive `pm clear`/cold-start cycles (unresponsive display, `mCurrentFocus=null`) — an environment issue, not a code one, given the identical mechanism already passed for Ukrainian and English and every locale file passed full static + structural verification. Flagged here rather than silently claimed as tested; live-checking `pl`/`ru` on a fresh emulator boot is a reasonable follow-up before considering this fully closed out.

**Explicitly out of scope, left for later:** threading the resolved app language into `localizeExercise()`'s call sites (exercise names/descriptions from the DB stay in whatever locale the caller already hardcodes — no `name_pl` column exists in the database either, a backend concern, not a frontend one); any change to `profiles.language`'s Supabase column definition (confirmed a free-text column already accepting `uk`/`ru` today, so `pl` needs no migration). The same "add Polish" request also covered the sister `CoreFit` web project, whose fixes are recorded in that project's own history, not here.

## Sprint 42 — Users search + Friends → Users discovery link (mobile)

**Context:** user request: Friends only ever showed already-added friends, with no path to the app's full user list except the bottom nav's Users tab — and that Users screen itself had no way to filter a long roster. Requested for both this project and `CoreFit` web in the same message; web's half is recorded in that project's own history.

**Reused rather than duplicated:** `src/components/exercise-search-bar.tsx` already implemented the exact input+clear-button UI needed, just hardcoded to an exercise-specific placeholder and name. Generalized it into `src/components/search-bar.tsx` (`SearchBar`, `placeholder` now a caller-supplied prop) instead of writing a second near-identical component — updates its two existing call sites (`exercise-picker.tsx`, `programs/wiki/index.tsx`) to pass their placeholder explicitly, no behavior change there. `users/index.tsx`'s new client-side filter (`useMemo` over `profile.username.toLowerCase().includes()`) mirrors the same pattern web's `wiki.tsx` already uses for exercise search (Sprint 41's audit), rather than inventing a new filtering approach.

**Friends → Users link:** `profile/friends.tsx` gets a new banner, styled identically to (but a separate style object from) the existing pending-requests banner just below it, shown only when `isOwnProfile` — browsing to add new friends doesn't belong on someone else's Friends screen, same gating the requests banner already uses.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error as every prior sprint), `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 195 base keys. Live verification was attempted via `expo start --web` rather than the Android emulator (Sprint 41 logged emulator instability under repeated cold starts, and this is a much smaller surface) — the app itself loaded and bundled correctly, but the Friends/Users screens require an authenticated session, and entering login credentials on the user's behalf isn't something this workflow does. Flagged here rather than silently claimed as tested; static checks are clean and the new code paths reuse already-verified patterns (`SearchBar`'s underlying UI was already live-tested pre-refactor, the filter logic mirrors an already-tested web equivalent), but an authenticated live pass is a reasonable follow-up.

## Sprint 43 — Friends/Requests/Users: floating subnav bar, replacing the Sprint 42 banners

**Context:** user showed a screenshot of the Friends screen (the "Find new friends" banner from Sprint 42, plus the friend list) and asked for a better navigation shape between Friends/Requests/Users — proposing either three link rows or tabs. Presented three concrete options (subnav pill bar / three stacked banners / single merged screen) via `AskUserQuestion` with ASCII previews; user picked the floating subnav bar, matching the recommendation.

**Reused rather than invented:** `FriendsSubNav` (`src/components/friends-sub-nav.tsx`) is a structural copy of the already-shipped `TrainingSubNav` (Complexes/Programs/Wiki) — same `BlurView` glass bar, same spring-animated active pill, same `getFloating*SubNavClearance`/`useFriendsChromeClearance` pairing pattern (mirrors `getFloatingSubNavClearance`/`useTrainingChromeClearance` exactly, kept as its own copy rather than a shared export since the two bars' geometry is only coincidentally identical). The one addition beyond `TrainingSubNav`'s shape: a small count badge on the "Requests" pill, reusing `Navigation`'s existing badge token (`#e5484d`, white bold text, `theme.workspace` border) repositioned for a text label instead of an icon.

**Route restructure required:** Friends/Requests already lived under `profile/_layout.tsx`, but Users was a sibling top-level route (`(app)/users/index.tsx`) — for one subnav bar to mount once and stay correct on all three screens (the same reason `TrainingSubNav` works: its whole route group *is* the three training screens), `users/index.tsx` moved to `profile/users.tsx`. Verified first that exactly one line in the whole codebase referenced `/users` (the Sprint 42 banner, removed in this same commit) — safe move. `profile/_layout.tsx` also serves `index`/`[id]`/`settings`, where a Friends subnav doesn't belong, so — unlike `programs/_layout.tsx`, which shows `TrainingSubNav` unconditionally — it renders `FriendsSubNav` only when `usePathname()` matches one of the three target routes.

**Accepted tradeoff, not engineered around:** that pathname check can't distinguish "my own Friends list" from "someone else's" (`/profile/friends?userId=X` is still just `/profile/friends`), so the subnav also appears while viewing another user's friends. Tapping Requests/Users from there goes to *your own* — there's no per-user route for either and shouldn't be one — and tapping Friends drops the `userId` param back to your own list.
