Judged low-harm; not worth duplicating `friends.tsx`'s `isOwnProfile` computation into the layout to special-case it.

**Cleanup:** both Sprint 42 banners (`browseUsersBanner`, `requestsBanner`) and their now-dead i18n keys (`profile.friends.browseUsers`, `profile.friends.pendingRequestsLabel`) removed along with the now-unused `useTheme`/`useFriendRequestsStore` reads in `friends.tsx` that only existed to feed them. New `components.friendsSubNav.{friends,requests,users}` keys added across all 4 locales — `friends`/`requests` duplicate `profile.friends.title`/`profile.requests.title`'s text (same precedent `trainingSubNav.wiki` already sets: subnav labels are their own short-word namespace, not aliases), `users` has no prior mobile equivalent and was reused verbatim from web's `base.users`. `users.tsx` gained a page title for the first time (previously had none) — reuses `components.friendsSubNav.users` directly as its `t()` call, the same convention `complexes/index.tsx` and `wiki/index.tsx` already follow.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing, unrelated error), `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 196 base keys. Live-tested this time — the user had their own Expo dev server already running (`localhost:8081`), so I drove it through Chrome directly rather than needing credentials: subnav renders on all three routes, correct route on tap, badge absent with zero pending requests (not independently confirmed >0, no pending requests existed in the test account), Users screen shows its new title and isn't obscured by the bar. **One quirk found and flagged, not fixed**: after a couple of rapid tab clicks, the *previously*-active pill's border sometimes stays visibly rendered alongside the new one instead of fading out, on Expo web specifically. Reproduced the identical behavior on the pre-existing `TrainingSubNav` under the same click pattern — inherited from the copied pattern, not introduced by this sprint's code, and unconfirmed on native (this session had no device/simulator access to check). Left as-is rather than expanding scope to debug an unrelated, already-shipped component.

**Follow-up same session:** user asked for two changes after seeing it live — Requests moved to first position (Friends stays visually centered, Users last), and confirmed the red request-count badge should stay. Also asked for the same subnav on `CoreFit` web. Web's `FriendsMenu` (`app/components/friendsMenu/`) mirrors `trainingMenu.tsx`'s exact structure and its narrow-only gating (`width >= 768` returns null) — desktop's `Navigation` is an in-flow top tab row there, not a floating bottom bar, so a fixed-position pill would float disconnected from the rest of desktop chrome at that width, same reasoning `TrainingMenu` already establishes. Required moving `friends/`, `friends/[id]/`, `requests/`, and `users/` into an `(social)` Next.js route group (URLs unchanged) so one shared layout could mount the bar across all three, the same restructuring shape this sprint already did on mobile. `allFriends.tsx`'s old banners now hide via a `max-width: 768px` media query rather than being deleted outright — deleting them would have left desktop with no path to Requests at all, since nothing else on desktop covers that gap. Recorded in this same entry rather than a new sprint number since it's a direct refinement of the same feature, not a new one.

## Sprint 44 — Records: top-3 weight leaderboard for a random exercise

**Context:** user asked for a "records" table — a Home-adjacent screen showing a random exercise (icon + name) and the top 3 users by heaviest weight logged for it, reached via a 2-item floating subnav on Home (Records left, Trainings center/default — the existing activity feed). Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: no third nav item, build for both mobile and web in this same pass (unlike some earlier features, not staged mobile-then-web).

**The real obstacle, found before any design decision:** weight isn't a structured number anywhere in this schema — `exercise_logs.weight` is a free-text `"XXX/YYxZ"` string (weight/reps×sets, confirmed against `workout-log-form.tsx`'s own placeholder, and against live data during testing: `"50/10x3"`), and no leaderboard/personal-record concept existed on either platform before this. Computing "who lifted the most" therefore isn't a query against existing structured columns.

**Decision: compute on demand via a new Postgres RPC, not a new table or a write-path change.** Rejected two alternatives: (a) adding structured `weight`/`reps` numeric columns — would touch `completeDay` on both platforms and need a backfill of existing history; (b) a `personal_bests` table updated at log time — same write-path risk, plus staleness if ever written to inconsistently. The RPC (`get_random_exercise_leaderboard()`, `supabase/migrations/20260816062432_add_get_random_exercise_leaderboard_function.sql` — this repo's *first* tracked migration file, applied to the live project via the already-authenticated `supabase` CLI, `db query --file ... --linked`) does it in one round trip: picks a random exercise among those with at least one row matching `^[0-9]+` at the start of `weight` (ignoring non-numeric entries like "bodyweight" rather than erroring on them), takes `MAX` of the parsed leading digits per user, returns the top 3 joined with exercise names/image and username/avatar. Verified live against production before writing any app code: two calls returned different random exercises with correctly-parsed weights and descending order.

`SECURITY DEFINER`, deliberately bypassing RLS — flagged explicitly as a considered tradeoff, not an oversight: a cross-user leaderboard *needs* to read other users' logs, and this isn't a new privacy exposure since the Home activity feed (`training_history`) is already fully cross-user-readable today. The function only ever returns an aggregate (max weight + username), never raw log rows.

**Routing, same shape as Sprint 43's `(social)` restructure:** neither `(app)/index.tsx` (mobile) nor `app/page.tsx` (web) had a `_layout.tsx` to mount a subnav into, unlike `profile/`/`programs`. Mobile: `(app)/index.tsx` → `(app)/(home)/index.tsx` (parenthesized group — doesn't affect the URL, `index.tsx` inside still resolves to `/`, the same trick `(social)` already uses on web) plus a new `(home)/_layout.tsx` and `(home)/records.tsx`. Unlike `FriendsSubNav`'s pathname gate (needed because `profile/_layout.tsx` serves unrelated screens too), `HomeSubNav` renders unconditionally — this group has exactly two screens and both want the bar.

**Reused rather than rebuilt:** `HomeSubNav` (`src/components/home-sub-nav.tsx`) is `FriendsSubNav` with the badge logic stripped and 2 items instead of 3. The Records screen's row (avatar + name + trailing weight, tap-through to `/profile/[id]`) is built from the same primitives `UserCard` uses (`Avatar`, `ThemedText`) rather than forcing `UserCard` itself, since `UserCard` requires a full `Profile` object (created_at, email, language, …) the leaderboard RPC never returns — fabricating those fields to satisfy the type would have been more misleading than writing four lines of layout. Exercise-name localization intentionally stays English-only (`localizeName()` in the new `records.ts`), matching every existing `localizeExercise()` call site in the app (Sprint 41 already logged this as a known, deliberately out-of-scope gap) — fixing it only for this one new screen would have been inconsistent, not an improvement.

**Functional testing:** RPC verified directly against production via CLI before any app code was written (two calls, two different random exercises, correct ordering). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing unrelated error), `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 200 base keys. Live-tested through the same running `localhost:8081` session as Sprint 43: Home shows the new subnav with Trainings active by default, Records tab loads a real exercise ("Cable Iron Cross standing") with a correctly-parsed weight (20) for a real test user, tapping back to Trainings returns to `/` correctly.

**Web half, same session:** `homeMenu.tsx` (narrow-only gated, same reasoning as `friendsMenu.tsx`), `app/page.tsx` → `app/(home)/page.tsx` (same route-group trick), `lib/recordsData.ts` calling the identical RPC — this repo's migration is the single source of truth for it, both apps share one Supabase project. One deliberate platform difference: web's `recordsData.ts` actually threads the current language through to `localizeName()` (matching `trainingData.ts`'s own `fetchExercisesByGroup` convention, which already does this), whereas mobile's `records.ts` stays English-only to match every other `localizeExercise()` call site there — same feature, each platform follows its own already-established localization convention rather than forcing parity between them. Verified live: `/records` and `/` both render correctly, correct exercise/weight/localized name, round-trip navigation back to Trainings works.

**Redesign, same session — v2:** user asked for a genuinely different shape after seeing v1 live: a *list* of exercises (10 by default, "Show more" loads 10 more) instead of one random pick, a muscle-group filter row at the top (reused directly from Wiki — `MuscleGroupFilter`/`WikiNav`, no new filter component on either platform), and a medal emoji (🥇🥈🥉) replacing the rank numeral. This isn't additive to the v1 RPC — `get_random_exercise_leaderboard()` is dropped and replaced by `get_exercise_leaderboards(muscle_group_id, limit, offset)` in the same migration file (`supabase/migrations/20260816080011_...`), since no screen used the old one anymore. Ordering switched from `random()` to `name_en` — pagination has to be stable across calls (offset 0 then offset 10 must not skip or repeat rows), which a random order can't guarantee. Verified the replacement the same way as v1: called the new RPC directly against production via CLI before touching app code — offset 0 and offset 10 returned disjoint exercise sets, the muscle-group filter narrowed correctly, and a follow-up query confirmed the old function no longer exists in `pg_proc`.

No pagination pattern existed anywhere in either codebase to mirror (confirmed by a targeted search first) — the offset/append `useState` + "Show more" button here is this app's first one, built from scratch rather than adapted from precedent.

**Mobile visual polish, three rounds, each driven by a live screenshot on the user's own device:**

1. *Card redesign + first tab-fit attempt.* Default 64px `MuscleGroupFilter` tabs only fit ~6 of 8 across one phone-width row, forcing a scroll the user didn't want; and each leaderboard entry had its own border, making the card look cluttered with a small exercise image. Fixed the card first (`records.tsx`): one border around the whole exercise block instead of one per row, exercise image grown 36px → 64px, entry rows made borderless/lighter underneath. For the tabs, added an opt-in `compact` prop to `MuscleGroupFilter` (icon-only, no label, small enough that all 8 fit one row) — used only on Records, so Wiki/exercise-picker keep the original 64px icon+label look.
2. *Labels restored, still one row.* Icon-only tabs "barely visible" per live feedback — impossible to tell muscle groups apart without a label. Brought the label back but kept the box narrow (42px wide, 20px icon, 9px font, `numberOfLines={2}`) so all 8 could still cram into one row. Same commit added the "kg" unit (translated in all 4 locales) after every weight value.
3. *Tried wrapping onto two rows — superseded within the same session.* A fresh screenshot showed round 2's labels still truncated to near-illegibility ("Should" was the only even partially-readable one), read at the time as "shrinking doesn't work, stop shrinking." Switched `compact` mode from a horizontal `ScrollView` to a `flexWrap: 'wrap'` grid at a larger, fully-legible size (76px, 26px icon). This technically fixed legibility but changed the wrong axis: the user's actual ask, on seeing it live, was the exact Wiki look — one row, horizontal scroll, no wrapping — not a wrapped grid. `compact` mode (and the prop itself) removed entirely; Records now calls `MuscleGroupFilter` with no props, rendering identically to Wiki's own filter row (single scrollable row, 64px tabs, full labels).
4. *Web-only clipping bug found while re-verifying round 3's revert.* With the single-row `ScrollView` restored, the row rendered visibly cropped in the browser preview — icons and labels cut off mid-glyph, well short of the tab's own 64px height. Cause: react-native-web's `View` defaults CSS `flex-shrink: 1` (native Yoga's default is `0`), so this fixed-height row — sitting above a `FlatList` inside `Workspace`'s `flex: 1` column — was being visually compressed to make room for the list below it, despite its explicit `height: 64`. Fixed with an explicit `flexShrink: 0` on the row's style. Native builds were presumably unaffected (Yoga's own default already matches), but this was only ever tested through the web preview in this session, so it's recorded as a web-specific fix rather than assumed harmless elsewhere.

Verified live at a phone-realistic (390px) viewport after both fixes: all 8 tabs render at full legible size in one row, scrolling reveals the rest (Biceps → Abs) exactly like Wiki's own filter, filter selection and refetch still work correctly.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx expo lint` clean except the one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error every prior sprint has also logged. No i18n keys changed across rounds 3–4, so the parity script wasn't re-run for them specifically (last clean at 200 base keys, round 2's `kg` key included).

5. *Final round: card density and tab icon spacing.* Per a fresh screenshot with dev-tools measurements attached: card `padding` measured 16px, read as too roomy — dropped to 8px (`Spacing.two`). Exercise name (17px) read as oversized relative to the rest of the card — dropped to 15px. Separately, the muscle-group icons in the filter tabs read as flush against the tab's own top border; added a one-sided `paddingTop: Spacing.one` to the tab so centered content sits with a visible gap from the top edge without disturbing the already-fine gap under the label. Verified live at 390px: card reads tighter, name is a size down, icons no longer touch the border.

**Web half, same session:** `recordsData.ts` rewritten for the same new RPC (still threading `lang` through, per the platform-specific convention already established for v1). `records.tsx` reuses `WikiNav` directly for the filter row rather than building a new one — `WikiNav` operates on muscle-group *name strings* ('Chest', 'Biceps', ...), not the RPC's `muscle_group_id`, so each filter change resolves through the already-existing `getMuscleGroupIdByName()` (`lib/trainingData.ts`) before calling the RPC, the same resolution `wiki.tsx` already does for its own filter. Live-verified: switching to Chest and then Biceps both returned the correct, visibly-matching exercise sets; "Show more" appended a second page of 10 with zero overlap with the first (confirmed by reading the full list, not just trusting the response). One process note: the browser-automation tool's synthetic clicks (both coordinate- and ref-based) didn't register on the "Show more" button — no network request fired — while a direct `element.click()` via injected JS did fire it correctly and produced the expected page-2 results; logged as an automation-environment quirk, not investigated further since the underlying handler is confirmed correct.

## Sprint 45 — Program cards: navy border replaced with an elevated shadow surface (mobile)

**Context:** user disliked `ProgramCard`'s bordered look (My programs, Complexes, another user's programs on their profile all share this one component) and asked whether something closer to Header/Navigation's shadow — rather than a border — would work, while flagging the exact right concern up front: might a plain shadow just blend into the background?

**Presented three concrete options as a built comparison page, not just described them** — a static HTML mockup reproducing the real program rows (Rak 6-d, global 5, Спп, 2-day Summer 2025) at the app's actual token values (`Colors.light`/`.dark`, `Spacing`, the Header/Navigation shadow recipe), with a light/dark toggle, published as an artifact rather than only described in chat:
- **A — Elevated:** border dropped entirely, filled with `backgroundElement` and lifted with the *exact* Header/Navigation shadow (`shadowColor:'#000', shadowOpacity:0.15, shadowRadius:16, shadowOffset:{0,6}, elevation:8`).
- **B — Shadow only:** the literal reading of "just add the header/nav shadow" — no fill change, no border. Confirmed the user's own suspicion live in the mockup: on dark theme the card nearly vanished, since Header/Navigation's own separation from content comes mainly from its `BlurView`, not the shadow — a flat card has nothing else to anchor it once the border's gone.
- **C — Hairline + soft lift:** swaps the heavy navy `border` token for `glassBorder` — already defined in `constants/theme.ts` specifically for "soften a hard edge on a floating surface," today only used on Header/Navigation's own edge — plus a lighter shadow.

User picked **A**. Implemented in `components/program-card.tsx`: `styles.card` drops `borderWidth`/`borderColor` entirely, gains `backgroundColor: theme.backgroundElement` and the same five shadow properties verbatim from `header.tsx`/`navigation.tsx` (kept as a literal copy, not a new shared constant — three call sites don't justify an extraction, and the header/nav pair already accepts this as a duplicated literal). Applies everywhere `ProgramCard` is used without any call-site changes, since the styling lives entirely in the shared component.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error). Live-verified on the running `localhost:8081` session at a phone-realistic (390px) viewport: My programs shows filled, shadowed cards with no border; Complexes (same component, plus its "Added" badge slot) confirmed unaffected by the style change and correctly positioned.

**Same-session correction, on-device:** the literal Header/Navigation shadow values read as "off" once seen on an actual device — that shadow is tuned for a bar with generous open space around it, but `ProgramsList`'s own list `gap` is only 8px between cards, far smaller than that shadow's ~22px spread (`offset:6 + radius:16`). The next card in the list physically covers most of the shadow, leaving only a thin grey sliver visible in the gap instead of a soft halo. Scaled the recipe down to actually fit an 8px gap (`shadowOpacity:0.12, shadowRadius:5, shadowOffset:{0,2}, elevation:3`) rather than reusing Header/Navigation's values verbatim — confirmed live that this reads as a proper soft-edged lift around the whole card instead of a line.

**Same-session extension: `UserCard` (Friends/Requests/Users).** User asked for the same treatment on the Friends list, shown a screenshot of the same bordered look. `UserCard` isn't built on `ProgramCard` (documented as a deliberate non-shared component — different content shape, no base "Card" to extract into), so the change is applied in parallel rather than inherited: border dropped, `backgroundColor: theme.backgroundElement` added, and the exact same already-fit-to-8px shadow recipe from the correction above (not Header/Navigation's original) — Friends, Requests, and Users all use the identical `Spacing.two` list gap `ProgramsList` does, so no separate tuning was needed. Live-verified on all three routes at 390px, including Users' `action` slot (Add friend / Cancel request / Remove friend), which renders unaffected alongside the new card style.

## Sprint 46 — Friends screen: chevron replaced with an inline "Remove friend" (mobile)

**Context:** looking at the elevated Friends list from Sprint 45's extension, user asked for the trailing chevron to become a "Remove friend" button instead — the row's own tap-through already reaches the profile, so a generic "more" arrow was redundant with an actual destructive action once one existed.

**Not the same shape as Users' own Remove friend.** Users screen (already had this) keeps the chevron *alongside* its action row (Add/Cancel/Remove/Pending), since those actions don't already imply "tap through" the way an arrow does. Friends explicitly asked to *replace* the arrow, not add beside it — `UserCard` gained a `hideChevron` prop (default `false`, every other call site unaffected) rather than bending Friends to match Users' layout just for consistency's sake.

**Reused the whole flow from `users.tsx`, not reinvented:** same `deleteFriendship` call, same `users.removeConfirm.title`/`.body` and `users.removeFriend` i18n keys (already generic enough to not be Users-screen-specific text), same `Platform.OS === 'web'` `window.confirm` vs. native `Alert.alert` split, same `submittingIds`/`actionError` shape — duplicated into `friends.tsx` rather than extracted into a shared hook, matching the precedent `requests.tsx` already set (it carries its own independent copy of this same submitting/error pattern rather than sharing one with `users.tsx`). One deliberate difference: `friends.tsx` doesn't retain the `profileById` map `resolveFriendProfiles` needs, so a successful removal triggers a full `fetchData` refetch rather than `users.tsx`'s lighter friendships-only `refreshFriendships`.

**Gated to `isOwnProfile`:** viewing someone else's friends list (reached via their `FriendsPreview`) stays read-only with the chevron intact — you can't remove another user's friends, so the action only ever renders on your own list, mirroring the gating this screen's title/empty-state copy already applies.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing, unrelated error), `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 202 base keys — no new keys needed, both reused strings already existed. Live-verified on `localhost:8081` at 390px: chevron gone, "Remove friend" renders in its place on both existing friends. The actual delete action wasn't exercised live — it would have removed a real friendship row from the shared dev database used as this whole session's test fixture — correctness here rests on reusing `users.tsx`'s already-live-tested `deleteFriendship` call verbatim, not a fresh live pass of the mutation itself.

**Same-session follow-up: themed `ConfirmDialog` replaces `Alert.alert`/`window.confirm`.** User explicitly required a confirmation step to exist (already did — see above) *and* that it visually match the rest of the app, not render as the OS/browser's own unstyled system dialog. No themed modal/dialog component existed anywhere in the codebase before this — every existing confirmation (program deletion, both `users.tsx` and this screen's own "Remove friend") used the native `Alert.alert`/`window.confirm` pair. New `components/confirm-dialog.tsx`: a `Modal` + backdrop `Pressable` (tap-outside dismisses, same as Cancel), styled as an elevated card using the same tokens as `ProgramCard`/`UserCard` (Sprint 45/46) — but the *original*, un-scaled-down Header/Navigation shadow recipe, since a centered modal has generous open space on every side, the exact case that shadow was sized for (unlike a tightly-packed list row). Two outlined `Button`s (Cancel / danger-bordered confirm), matching the app's uniform outlined-button language rather than introducing a filled accent color. Wired into both `friends.tsx`'s and `users.tsx`'s "Remove friend" flow — replaces the `Platform.OS === 'web'` branch entirely (no more Alert/window.confirm split needed at all, on either screen) via a `pendingRemoval` state object instead of directly invoking a native dialog.

**Bug caught and fixed live, not by inspection:** after wiring it up, tapping Cancel visibly cleared the dialog's content (name interpolation went blank on next render, confirming state genuinely reset) but the dialog's card stayed on screen instead of disappearing. Root cause: `Modal`'s `visible={false}` doesn't actually hide/unmount its content on react-native-web — a real platform limitation, not an automation-click artifact (confirmed via a native DOM click listener showing the click landed and bubbled normally). Fixed by having `ConfirmDialog` return `null` when its own `visible` prop is false, rather than passing that prop through to `Modal` and trusting it to hide itself — the `Modal` is now mounted only while actually needed. Re-verified live on both `friends.tsx` and `users.tsx`: dialog opens with the correct name/email interpolated, Cancel now fully closes it, confirmed via a DOM query for the dialog's own text rather than a screenshot alone.

## Sprint 47 — Trainer/client relationships: request-a-friend-as-trainer, remote program editing

**Context:** two related asks in one message: a profile screen (`profile/[id].tsx`) with no friend action at all (had to leave the screen and go to Users to add/remove a friend); and a new relationship type — from a friend's profile, invite them to become *your* trainer. Confirmed direction explicitly before building anything: the viewer is the client, inviting the profile they're looking at to be their trainer; the trainer then confirms the viewer is their client. Once accepted, the trainer can edit that client's program structure remotely. Given the size (new table, new RLS, a permission model letting one user write another's data), this went through `EnterPlanMode` rather than being implemented directly — plan approved before any code was written, mobile-only for this pass (not web).

**Research before design, all confirmed live via `supabase db query --linked`, not assumed:** `programs` has RLS **disabled entirely** — the app's `.eq('user_id', userId)` filters are the only protection today, which meant no new policy was needed there, only on `program_days`/`program_exercises` (matches the RLS gap already tracked since Sprint 21/32). `friends` has no unique constraint on a pair — the app already resolves relationship state via `.find()` over the full two-directional list, a pattern worth mirroring exactly rather than reinventing. `profiles.is_trainer` already exists but is a *different*, unrelated concept (an admin-managed flag gating global-program authoring on web, confirmed by reading both apps' actual usage) — the new table doesn't touch it, and this sprint's "trainer" terminology is a deliberate second use of the word for a different feature, not a conflict.

**`trainer_clients` table + RLS** (`supabase/migrations/20260816132458_add_trainer_clients.sql`): mirrors `friends`' shape (`id`/`status`/`created_at`) but with named, direction-specific columns (`trainer_id`/`client_id`) rather than `friends`' symmetric `user_id`/`friend_id`, since direction is meaningful here in a way it isn't for friends. RLS deliberately scoped tighter than `friends`' own original `SELECT true` policy from the start (`trainer_id = auth.uid() or client_id = auth.uid()`) — Maintenance 01 already showed that pattern needs walking back once, so this table starts where `friends` ended up rather than repeating the mistake. The INSERT policy enforces "only among already-accepted friends" via an `EXISTS` against `friends` in its own `with check` — a real business rule enforced at the DB layer, not just the UI. Three separate DELETE policies (client cancels own pending / trainer declines incoming pending / either party removes an accepted link) — deliberately written as three from the start, applying the exact lesson `friends`' Maintenance 01/02 had to learn the hard way (a missing decline-policy silently no-op'd in production before that fix).

**Program-edit extension:** new, additive INSERT/DELETE policies on `program_days`/`program_exercises` granting an accepted trainer access scoped through `trainer_clients` — kept as separate named policies rather than editing the existing owner-only ones, for an independently revertible diff (mirrors the shape `program_days`' own INSERT policy already uses for global-program authors, just as a new policy instead of a new `OR` branch in an existing one). Every RLS boundary — friendship-gated insert (and its rejection when not friends), wrong-party accept (0 rows, not an error), all three DELETE cases, trainer insert/delete on a client's `program_days`/`program_exercises` vs. a non-trainer's rejected attempt — verified live via `set local role authenticated; set local request.jwt.claims = ...` impersonation *before* any app code was written, each wrapped in a rolled-back transaction so nothing persisted in the shared dev database.

**New `src/lib/supabase/trainer-clients.ts`**, mirroring `friends.ts` function-for-function: `getTrainerClientLinksForUser`, `getTrainerClientState` (same `none/outgoing/incoming/accepted` shape as `getFriendshipState`), `sendTrainerRequest`, `deleteTrainerLink` (one function for cancel/decline/remove, same reasoning `deleteFriendship` already established), `acceptTrainerRequest`, `getIncomingTrainerRequests`, and one addition with no `friends.ts` equivalent — `isAcceptedTrainerOfClient(trainerId, clientId)`, a single targeted query rather than fetching the whole relationship list, used only by the program-edit permission check below.

**`profile/[id].tsx`:** gained the friend action block it never had (Add/Remove friend, reusing `getFriendshipState`/`ConfirmDialog` exactly as `users.tsx` already does) plus, only once friends, a trainer action block (Add as trainer / Trainer request sent / Your trainer / Your client) — the "Your client" label and its own confirm copy exist because the relationship is asymmetric and either party can end it, resolved locally by checking which side of the matching link the viewer is on rather than teaching `getTrainerClientState` about display concerns. Requires a second `getFriendshipsForUser` call (the *viewer's* own friendships — a different query from the existing one, which fetches the *viewed* profile's friends for `FriendsPreview`).

**`requests.tsx`:** new "Trainer requests" section below Friend Requests, plain fetch-on-focus (no store/Realtime — matches how Friend Requests itself started before Sprint 35's realtime follow-up; easy to promote the same way later if needed). Caught two real bugs while verifying this live, neither an automation artifact: (1) stacking a second, independently-scrolling `FlatList` below the first inside a non-scrolling `Workspace` left overflow content unreachable — fixed by switching this screen to `Workspace`'s `scroll` mode with two plain mapped sections instead of FlatLists (neither list is ever long enough to need virtualization); (2) even after that fix, the section still didn't render — traced via temporary `console.log`s (not guessed) to a Zod `UUID` validation failure, itself caused by a malformed placeholder UUID (`11111111-…`) used in a manual SQL seed for testing, which fails Zod's RFC4122 variant-nibble check that a real `gen_random_uuid()` value always satisfies — not an application bug, but real enough to have hidden the actual FlatList/scroll issue underneath it until debugged properly.

**Program edit, extended not rebuilt:** `programs/[id].tsx`'s `isOwner` was used in exactly 3 places — Delete icon, Edit icon, and whether to show the interactive `WorkoutLogForm` vs. a read-only exercise list. Only the Edit icon's gate changed (`isOwner || isAcceptedTrainer`); Delete and workout logging stay owner-only by deliberate scope decision (a trainer edits structure, never deletes the program or logs workouts on the client's behalf) — proposed as the recommended default alongside the rest of the plan (user engaged with the direction-of-invite question specifically and left the remaining defaults, including this one, unobjected) rather than separately confirmed. `isAcceptedTrainer` is checked once per screen load via `isAcceptedTrainerOfClient`, only when `!isOwner` (no extra query on the common case). `create.tsx`'s edit-mode save previously assumed the logged-in user was always the program's owner (`updateProgramStructure(programId, user.id, ...)`); now captures the program's actual `user_id` from `getProgramDetail` at prefill time and passes that instead — the one line that makes a trainer's save actually target their client's row rather than (harmlessly, since the RLS-filtered update would just match 0 rows) failing silently.

**Functional testing:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing, unrelated error), `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 212 base keys. Live-verified the full loop on `localhost:8081`, all through seeded-then-cleaned-up rows (never a real click on native Alert-free flows against production-shaped data left behind): sent a trainer request from a friend's profile, saw "Cancel request" render and correctly cancel; seeded an incoming request directly, confirmed it rendered on Requests with the right name, accepted it via a real UI click, confirmed the DB row moved to `accepted` (not deleted); viewed the now-client's profile and confirmed "Your client" rendered (correct role direction, not "Your trainer"); as the accepted trainer, opened one of the client's real programs and confirmed the Edit pencil now shows while Delete stays hidden and the workout log stays read-only, then opened the edit wizard and confirmed it prefilled with the client's real program data. The actual structural save wasn't exercised through the full multi-step wizard UI (too fragile/risky to automate against shared dev-account data) — correctness there rests on the already-live-verified RLS policies plus the now-correct `programOwnerId` being threaded through, not a fresh click-through of every wizard step.

**Same-session follow-up: gate trainer requests by `profiles.is_trainer`.** User asked for two more rules once the base feature was live: only an actual trainer can *receive* a "be my trainer" request, and a trainer can't *send* one themselves (can't also be someone else's client). `profiles.is_trainer` already existed — "backend/admin-managed", gating only web's global-program authoring — asked the user directly whether to reuse it or add a new column, flagging the real cross-feature side effect first: reusing it means toggling "I'm a trainer" for this feature also grants global-program authoring on web, and vice versa. User chose reuse, and Settings (not the profile screen) for the self-service toggle, both the recommended options.

`profiles`' own UPDATE RLS (`auth.uid() = id`, no column restriction) already permitted a user to self-edit `is_trainer` — confirmed live, not assumed from the old comment — so only `profile.ts`'s client-side `ProfileUpdate` type needed to stop excluding it; no RLS change on `profiles` itself. `trainer_clients`' INSERT policy extended via `ALTER POLICY ... WITH CHECK` (in place, not a drop/recreate) to also require `coalesce(target.is_trainer, false) = true` and `coalesce(requester.is_trainer, false) = false`. Both accounts already used for testing this whole sprint happened to already be `is_trainer = true` — a real, not contrived, positive test case for "a trainer can't send a request" (confirmed rejected), then temporarily flipped one to `false` inside a rolled-back transaction to confirm the positive path (non-trainer inviting an actual trainer succeeds) and the remaining negative path (neither side a trainer), before restoring the original value.

New Settings toggle ("I am a trainer") — same instant-apply shape and pill styling as the existing Theme toggle immediately above it, with a hint line disclosing the global-programs side effect at the point of toggling, not just in a code comment. `profile/[id].tsx` gained one more fetch (the *viewer's* own profile, for their `is_trainer`; the *viewed* profile's own flag was already available from the existing `getProfileById` call) and a new gate on the "Add as trainer" button only — an already-outgoing or already-accepted relationship stays visible regardless of either party's current flag, matching the same "existing state isn't retroactively affected" reasoning the base feature already established for friendship gating.

Live-verified end to end: with the viewer already `is_trainer = true`, "Add as trainer" correctly did not render on a friend's profile; toggled it off via the real Settings UI, confirmed the DB row actually changed, confirmed the button reappeared on the same friend's profile; restored the original value afterward so the shared dev account wasn't left in a different state than before.

**Same-session polish: matching section headings on Requests.** Once Friend Requests and Trainer requests existed side by side, the asymmetry stood out on a real screenshot — "Trainer requests" had its own heading, the friend-requests section above it didn't (only the page title "Requests"). Added a "Friend requests" heading using the exact same `ThemedText` type and left alignment as "Trainer requests", rather than centering either to match the page title — a page title and a section heading are different levels of hierarchy, so matching the two *section* headings to each other was the right read of "make them consistent," not making one of them look like the page title.

## Sprint 48 — Place of residence: self-hosted city autocomplete + auto-detect (mobile + web)

**Context:** Settings gained a "place of residence" field — a text input with live autocomplete as the user types, plus an auto-detect-my-location option. User explicitly delegated the technical approach ("I think you know better how to implement this") but was asked the one highest-impact question directly: where the city data comes from. **Chose a self-hosted Supabase table (~600 major world cities, weighted toward Ukraine/Russia/Poland/English-speaking countries) over an external geocoding API** — no API keys, no third-party dependency, autocomplete and auto-detect both resolve entirely offline against this own table.

**`cities` table + `nearest_city` RPC** (`supabase/migrations/20260818110000_add_cities_and_profile_location.sql`): `id/name/country/country_code/lat/lng/population`, RLS scoped to public `SELECT` only — deliberately no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy at all (a reference table has no legitimate client-write case, unlike `trainer_clients`, which at least needed a scoped `SELECT`). `pg_trgm` + a GIN index on `name` backs the `ilike 'query%'` prefix search; a `population desc` index breaks ties toward the more useful result when several cities share a prefix. `nearest_city(lat, lng)` uses a proper haversine formula rather than naive squared-degree distance — this app's primary user base (Ukraine/Russia/Poland, per its 4 supported languages) sits at mid-to-high latitudes where a degree of longitude covers noticeably less real-world distance than a degree of latitude, so the naive approach would measurably mis-rank nearby cities. `profiles` gained plain `city text` / `country text` columns, denormalized with no FK into `cities.id` — matches this schema's existing pattern of avoiding joins in profile reads.

**Seed data** (~618 cities, loaded via a one-off Python-generated SQL insert, not committed as a script): built from well-known public geographic facts (national capitals + major cities by population), not scraped from any proprietary source. Coverage is deliberately uneven by design — dense for Ukraine (~60 cities down to district-town size), Russia (~90), Poland (~50), and English-speaking countries (~350 across the US/UK/Canada/Australia/Ireland/NZ/South Africa/India), lighter (1-3 cities) for the remaining ~150 countries. Population figures are rounded approximations used only for search ranking, not display precision. Every RLS/RPC boundary was verified live via `supabase db query --linked` before any application code was written — matches this session's established order for every prior RLS/RPC change: anonymous `SELECT` succeeds (618 rows), an `authenticated`-role `INSERT` correctly fails with `42501` (no policy), `ilike 'Ky%'` returns Kyiv ranked above Kyoto by population, and `nearest_city` returns the exact seeded city for Kyiv/London/Warsaw's real coordinates.

**Both platforms got a hand-rolled autocomplete, not a third-party widget** — despite the user explicitly offering "a good widget or library" as an option. Reasoned as more consistent with each app's own architecture: mobile's `Button`/`SearchBar`/`MuscleGroupFilter`/`UserCard` are all custom components built directly against theme tokens, and web's own inputs are hand-styled CSS Modules with MUI used only once, in Complexes' Accordion — a mismatched Autocomplete widget would have stood out on both platforms. Suggestions render as a plain inline list under the input (not an absolutely-positioned overlay) on both platforms — Settings already scrolls, and an overlay would only risk z-index/clipping fights against that scroll container for no real benefit at this list size.

**Mobile**: `src/lib/supabase/cities.ts` (`searchCities`, `getNearestCity`, mirroring `profile.ts`'s shape) plus `city`/`country` added to `profile.ts`'s schema, `PROFILE_COLUMNS`, and `ProfileUpdate`. New `src/components/city-select.tsx` — a debounced (250ms, hand-rolled `setTimeout`, no library) text input + inline suggestion list + a "detect automatically" action using `expo-location`'s `getCurrentPositionAsync`, newly installed and registered in `app.config.ts`'s `plugins` with a `locationWhenInUsePermission` string. Wired into `settings.tsx` as a new section between Trainer and Language, same instant-apply-on-select shape as the existing Theme toggle.

**Web**: `lib/citiesData.ts` mirrors `userData.ts`'s own conventions (never throws, returns `[]`/`null`, fresh `createClient()` per call) and uses the browser's native `navigator.geolocation` for auto-detect — no new package needed. `types/user.ts`'s `User`/`UserSettings` and `userData.ts`'s `fetchUserSettings`/`updateUserProfile` extended with `city`/`country`. New `app/settings/components/citySettings.tsx` reuses `settings.module.scss`'s shared `.languages, .profile, .trainer` block by adding `.location` to the same selector group (the same pattern `.trainer` itself was added by), with its own small `&__input`/`&__dropdown`/`&__suggestion`/`&__detect` rules layered on top. Wired into `settings.tsx` between `TrainerSettings` and `LanguagesBox`. i18n keys (`settings.locationSection`, `.cityPlaceholder`, `.detectLocation`, `.noMatches`, `.locationError`) added identically to all 4 language blocks in `lib/languages.ts` plus the English default in `store/language-slice.ts`'s `initialState`.

**A real lint issue caught by `expo lint`, not by inspection:** the first draft of `city-select.tsx` called `setState` synchronously inside two `useEffect` bodies (re-seeding the input text when the persisted profile value changes; clearing stale suggestions when the query got too short) — flagged by the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule as a cascading-render risk. Fixed by moving the re-seed to React's own recommended "adjust state during render" pattern (a guarded conditional `setState` call directly in the component body, not inside `useEffect`) and by deriving the "hide suggestions" condition at render time from `query.trim().length` instead of synchronously resetting state for it — the debounced-search effect itself now only ever calls `setState` from inside its `setTimeout`/promise callbacks, never directly in the effect body. `npx expo lint` clean afterward except the one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error every prior sprint has also logged; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms; `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 223 base keys.

**Live-verified on web** at `localhost:3000/settings` (already-logged-in session): typing "Ky" surfaced "Kyiv, Ukraine" and "Kyoto, Japan" ranked by population; selecting Kyiv instant-applied with no separate Save step; a full page reload confirmed the value persisted to the database rather than just local state. **Not live-verified on mobile in this pass** — the running Expo web dev server had no active login session available to this tool, and entering credentials on the user's behalf is out of scope; mobile's correctness here rests on the identical `tsc`/lint-clean code path and the same already-verified RLS/RPC layer underneath, not a fresh click-through. **Also not automated on either platform**: the actual OS/browser geolocation permission prompt itself (a different category from JS `alert()/confirm()`) — the `nearest_city` RPC logic behind it was verified directly via SQL instead; the user should personally exercise the full tap-to-permission-to-detected-city flow at least once on each platform.

**Same-session follow-up: native-script search.** User reported typing "хмель" found nothing, despite Khmelnytskyi being a real, sizeable Ukrainian city. Two distinct bugs, not one: (1) Khmelnytskyi (an oblast capital, ~274k population) was genuinely missing from the seed — a real gap in what was supposed to be full Ukraine oblast-capital coverage, fixed by adding it plus Kamianets-Podilskyi. (2) The deeper structural bug: `cities.name` is Latin-only ("Khmelnytskyi"), and search matched only that column — a Cyrillic-script query could never match *any* city, not just this one, since the scripts don't overlap at all. This affects every Ukrainian- or Russian-speaking user typing their own language, i.e. this app's primary audience.

Fixed with new `name_uk`/`name_ru` columns (`supabase/migrations/20260818130633_add_city_native_names_search.sql`), search-only — display and the value written to `profiles.city`/`country` still always use the canonical English `name`, deliberately unchanged, to avoid a "just selected in Ukrainian" vs. "reloaded and shows English" inconsistency that localizing display would have introduced without also re-architecting how the persisted value round-trips. Populated for Ukraine and Russia in full (matching the seed's own stated weighting toward this app's primary audience) plus a shortlist of ~50 other globally recognizable capitals/metros (London, Paris, Berlin, New York, Tokyo, ...) — not all ~620 seeded cities, the same deliberately uneven coverage philosophy the original seed already used.

New `search_cities(query)` RPC matches across `name`/`name_uk`/`name_ru` server-side (a parameterized `ilike ... || '%'` inside the function body, not a client-built `.or()` filter string) — centralizes the three-column OR once rather than duplicating it in both `cities.ts` and `citiesData.ts`, and sidesteps any PostgREST filter-string escaping concerns entirely. Both platforms' `searchCities` swapped from `.from('cities').ilike('name', ...)` to `.rpc('search_cities', { query })`; `getNearestCity`/`nearest_city` untouched (unaffected by this bug — auto-detect was never keyboard input).

Verified directly via SQL before touching app code (established SOP): `search_cities('хмель')` → Khmelnytskyi; `search_cities('Лондон')` → London; `search_cities('Ky')` → Kyiv/Kyoto unchanged (confirms the English-prefix path wasn't regressed). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms. Live-verified on web: typing "хмель" now surfaces "Khmelnytskyi, Ukraine" in the suggestion list.

**Same-session follow-up: localized display/storage.** User asked directly whether the picked city could be shown "in the app's own languages" rather than always English ("Khmelnytskyi, Ukraine" while the rest of the Ukrainian-language UI was in Ukrainian read as inconsistent). Both `nearest_city` and `search_cities` RPCs (`supabase/migrations/20260818131226_return_city_native_names.sql`, drop+recreate since `RETURNS TABLE`'s column list changed) now also return `name_uk`/`name_ru`. A new `resolveCityLabel(city, lang)` in both `cities.ts` (mobile) and `citiesData.ts` (web) mirrors `getExerciseName`'s existing per-language fallback shape exactly: Ukrainian/Russian use their own column when the city has one, English/Polish fall back to the canonical `name` (Polish has no dedicated column here either, same precedent). Country names are a small `country_code → name` lookup covering only the country codes that already have at least one translated city — not a per-city column, which would have repeated (and risked drifting) across every city row sharing a country.

`CitySelect`/`CitySettings` resolve the label at selection time (from either a typed suggestion or the detect-location result) using the current app language — mobile reads `i18n.language`, web reads the `getLanguage` selector, both validated against their own known-language-code union with an English fallback for anything unexpected. The *resolved* label, not the raw English city, is what gets both displayed and written to `profiles.city`/`country` — a deliberate choice over storing English-always-and-relocalizing-on-display, since this field has no other consumer today and re-localizing an already-stored plain-text value on a later language switch isn't attempted anywhere else in the app either (e.g. `username`). Live-verified on web: typing "хмель" while in Ukrainian now shows "Хмельницький, Україна" (both city and country localized) in the suggestion list; selecting it and reloading confirmed that exact Ukrainian string persisted to the database.

## Sprint 49 — City displayed on both profile pages (mobile + web)

**Context:** the city/country picked in Settings (Sprint 48) had no other consumer anywhere in the app — asked to surface it on the profile page itself, on both platforms.

`profiles.city`/`.country` were already selected by every profile fetch mobile uses (`profile.ts`'s `PROFILE_COLUMNS`), so `profile/index.tsx` (own profile) and `profile/[id].tsx` (another user's) both needed only a JSX addition, no data-layer change — a `{profile.city}{profile.country ? \`, ${profile.country}\` : ''}` line placed right after the joined-date line in each, hidden entirely when unset (same "hide, don't show empty" convention `PeoplePreview` already established), reusing each file's own existing dimmed-secondary-text style for the date rather than inventing a new one.

Web's server-side `getOwnProfile`/`getProfileById` (`lib/data/user.ts`) did *not* already select `city`/`country` — `ProfileType` never needed them before this — so both got added there and to `ProfileType` itself (`types/user.ts`). `Profile.client.tsx` (the one shared component behind both `/profile` and `/profile/[id]` on web, unlike mobile's two separate screens) gained a second `<span>` after the joined-date one. Caught before it shipped: `.profile__header`'s existing `span{opacity:0.4}` rule dims by type selector, but a bare `<span>` is inline — a second one right after the first would have flowed onto the *same* line as the date instead of its own, since nothing forces a break between two adjacent inline elements. Added a `.profile__city{display:block}` class (new selector, `.profile__header span{opacity:0.4}` still applies to it via the same descendant-type-selector match, so it's dimmed *and* block) rather than reworking the existing date span's own styling.

Live-verified on web at `localhost:3000/profile` (already-logged-in session, own account already carrying "Хмельницький, Україна" from Sprint 48's own live-testing): renders correctly on its own line, dimmed to match the joined-date line above it. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms; `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated error every prior sprint has logged); `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 223 base keys (no new keys — both lines reuse data already fetched, no new translated strings). Mobile's own rendering wasn't separately live-verified in the browser (same no-active-login-session limitation as Sprint 48) — correctness rests on the identical `tsc`-clean JSX pattern already used for the joined-date line right next to it.

## Sprint 50 — Records skeleton restyled to match the Trainings/history feed (mobile + web)

**Context:** explicit style-unification request — Records' loading skeleton should look like the Trainings/history feed's own skeleton (web's `NewsSkeleton`, mobile's `HomeCardSkeleton` in `(home)/index.tsx`), on both platforms. Not a fidelity fix: Records' real `.card`/`records.tsx` card is border-only (`border: 1px solid var(--border-color)`), and confirmed that's *still* what it looks like — this only changes the skeleton's own visual language to a filled `--skeleton-wrapper-bg`/`theme.skeletonWrapperBg` card with no border, matching News/HomeCardSkeleton exactly, deliberately diverging from its own real card's border treatment the way News' skeleton already does from its own.

Card container: `border-radius:8px + 1px border` → `border-radius:4px + skeleton-wrapper-bg fill, no border` on both platforms (web: `ui/skeleton/skeleton.module.scss`'s `.recordsPage__card`; mobile: `records-skeleton.tsx`'s `styles.card`). Row-level placeholders were rebuilt rather than reused as-is: the old skeleton had one generic 60%-width bar per card; this mirrors `records.tsx`'s real `EntryRow` (rank/avatar/name/weight, 2 rows per card) instead of collapsing to a News-style single bar-per-line — a leaderboard still reads as a leaderboard shape as a placeholder, even though the *card wrapper* now matches News' style exactly.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms; `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated error every prior sprint has logged). Live-verified on web via the established force-hack-then-revert pattern (`if (true || loading) return <RecordsSkeleton/>`, screenshotted, then reverted and confirmed no leftover hack via `git diff`/grep): three filled cards render with no border, each showing an exercise image+name header and two rank/avatar/name/weight entry rows. Mobile's own rendering wasn't live-verified — the running Expo web dev server (port 8082) had no active login session available to this tool, same limitation as Sprints 48/49 — correctness rests on `records-skeleton.tsx` using the identical `theme.skeletonWrapperBg` token already proven correct in `HomeCardSkeleton` right next to it in the same app.

## Sprint 51 — Complexes: web-matching expand/collapse accordions (mobile)

**Context:** mobile's Complexes list tapped through to a separate full-screen detail route (`programs/complexes/[id].tsx`) for each global program; web instead expands each program in place as an accordion (MUI `<Accordion>`, uncontrolled — no `expanded`/`onChange` passed, so several can be open at once, not exclusively one at a time). Asked to bring mobile's visual behavior in line with web's exactly, same icons included.

**New `GlobalProgramAccordion`** (inline in `complexes/index.tsx`, not a separate shared component — the only place this pattern exists in the app): each list item owns its own `expanded` boolean and detail-fetch state independently, matching web's uncontrolled-per-item behavior rather than a single shared "one open at a time" selection. Detail (days/exercises) is fetched lazily via the already-existing `getGlobalProgramDetail(id)` on first expand and cached in local state — not fetched upfront for every program in the list the way web's own `fetchGlobalProgramsWithDetails` does; a mobile-appropriate adaptation of the same visual result (nothing the user can see differs), not a difference. Exercise names/images resolve through a `Map<id, LocalizedExercise>` built once from `getExercises()` alongside the initial list fetch, mirroring `programs/[id].tsx`'s own established pattern for the same lookup — including calling `localizeExercise(exercise)` with no explicit locale, matching every other screen in the app that resolves exercise names today (all of them currently default to English regardless of app language; out of scope to change here, this task was visual/behavioral parity, not an i18n audit).

**Chevron icon**: a new `ExpandIcon`, `react-native-svg` with MUI's actual `ExpandMoreIcon` path data (`M16.59 8.59L12 13.17 7.41 8.59 6 10l6 6 6-6z`, the standard Material "expand_more" glyph) rather than an approximated system icon — rotated 180° via a conditional `transform` when expanded, the same CSS-transform approach MUI's own Accordion uses, not a second swapped-in "collapse" icon.

**Card/row visuals measured from web's actual current styles** (`complexes.tsx`/`complexes.module.scss`), not estimated: bordered card (`theme.border`, matching the app's own theme-aware convention rather than porting web's hardcoded `#204879` literally), title + "Type: X | Level: Y" secondary line reusing already-existing `programs.byId.typeLabel`/`.levelLabel` i18n keys (no new keys needed anywhere in this sprint), "Day N" heading per day (`programs.day`, already used by the now-removed detail screen), and 40×40 exercise-row images/names matching web's own explicit `width="40" height="40"` — not Wiki's 64×64 convention, since this is a visual-parity task specifically, not a generic list-row pattern.

`complexes/[id].tsx` deleted — confirmed via grep with no remaining references anywhere in the app, its only purpose (viewing a global program's days/exercises) is now fully covered by the inline accordion, and Expo Router's file-based routing would otherwise have kept it reachable as dead, unreferenced UI.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error every prior sprint has logged). Live-verified on `localhost:8081` (already-logged-in session): expanding "global 5" rotated its chevron, showed "Day 1" with three exercise rows (image+name) and a correctly-labeled "Remove from my programs" button (this program is already owned by the test account); expanding "gloval 4" alongside it (without collapsing the first) showed its own independent five-exercise day and a correctly-labeled "Add to my programs" button (not owned) — confirms both the per-item independent expand state and the owned/not-owned button wiring match web.

**Same-session follow-up: 1px button border, clipped corners, smooth open/close.** Three fixes from live feedback on the freshly-built accordion:

1. **Button border** — the "Add/Remove from my programs" button used `Button`'s default `borderWidth: 2`; overridden to `1` for this one instance via the existing `style` prop escape hatch (`styles.actionButton`), not a change to `Button` itself, which every other call site in the app still uses at 2px correctly.
2. **Clipped bottom corners** — `styles.card` had `borderRadius` but no `overflow: 'hidden'`, so the card's own rounded bottom corners rendered flattened once expanded (the content block's square edges sit right at the boundary). Added `overflow: 'hidden'` — the standard fix for this exact symptom in React Native/RN Web, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` on the live page rather than just visually: both the collapsed and expanded card correctly report `overflow: "hidden"` and `borderRadius: "8px"` after the fix.
3. **Smooth expand/collapse** — RN has no native `height: auto` transition, so this needed the standard measure-then-animate pattern: content stays permanently mounted (not conditionally rendered) once a first expand triggers its detail fetch, its natural height captured via `onLayout` and cached in state; a `react-native-reanimated` shared value (`withTiming`, 220ms, `Easing.out(Easing.cubic)` — already a project dependency, used elsewhere for Navigation's pill animations) animates between `0` and that measured height on every toggle, with the chevron rotation driven by the same animation config for a synchronized feel. The very first expand grows in two visible steps (a small "loading…" height, then the full content height once the fetch resolves and `onLayout` re-fires with the real size) — every later toggle already knows its target height and animates directly to it in one motion.

Verified via `getComputedStyle` queries against the live `localhost:8081` page (not just screenshots, since the automation tooling's zoom-region coordinates proved unreliable for pixel-level corner inspection in this session) and a console-error check across a full collapse→expand cycle (none). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated error).

**Same-session follow-up: danger color for the remove action.** The "Remove from my programs" state of the button read identically to "Add to my programs" (same border/text color) — no visual distinction for the destructive direction. Fixed by conditionally applying `theme.danger` to both the border and text only when `ownedProgramId` is set, mirroring `friends.tsx`'s own already-established "Remove friend" button exactly (`style={[styles.removeButton, { borderColor: theme.danger }]}` + `themeColor="danger"` on the label) rather than inventing a new pattern. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; live-verified on `localhost:8081` — the button now renders with a red border and red label specifically when removing, while "Add to my programs" on the still-unowned "gloval 4" stays the normal border/text color.

**Same-session follow-up: content should scroll full-height behind the floating header/footer, matching Home.** The "Complexes" title was a static, non-scrolling sibling rendered *above* the FlatList (`marginTop: clearance.top`) — this caps the list's own frame at the title's bottom edge, so it can never scroll its top content up behind the floating header the way `(home)/index.tsx`'s own feed does. `Workspace`'s own doc comment already states the correct pattern explicitly: clearance belongs on the scrollable content's own `contentContainerStyle` as internal padding, not as a margin on a sibling sitting above it.

Moved the title into the FlatList's `ListHeaderComponent`, with `paddingTop: clearance.top` (alongside the already-correct `paddingBottom: clearance.bottom`) on its `contentContainerStyle` — so the list's frame now spans the full available height, top to bottom, with clearance as content padding rather than an external boundary. The loading/error/empty (non-list) states didn't need this, matching Home's own same split: only the *actual* scrollable state gets internal padding, everything else keeps a plain `marginTop: clearance.top` wrapper — a skeleton, error message, or "no programs" text isn't something a user scrolls behind chrome anyway.

Verified structurally rather than by eyeballing pixels (this session's zoom-region screenshots proved unreliable for this kind of check): queried the live page's DOM directly and confirmed the scrollable container's own bounding rect now starts at `y≈8` — essentially the safe-area top edge, the same origin the floating header itself renders at — rather than below the title as before. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean (same one pre-existing, unrelated error).

## Sprint 52 — Complete button disabled without a date or any logged value (mobile + web)

**Context:** the per-day workout-log "Complete"/"Завершити" button could be pressed with no date and/or every exercise field still blank. Mobile's `WorkoutLogForm` already guarded against a missing date (`disabled={!date || ...}`); web's `TrainingProcessing` had no guard at all — `disabled={submittedDays[index]}` only. Neither checked whether the user had actually logged anything for that day.

**Mobile** (`components/workout-log-form.tsx`): added `hasAnyValueEntered` (derived from `values` state, reactive for the button's `disabled` prop) and a ref-based `hasAnyValue()` twin used inside `handleComplete`'s own guard, consistent with every other read of in-progress input in this file already preferring the ref over `values` state at submit time (see its own long-standing comment on why: onBlur carries no payload, and a closure over `values` isn't guaranteed to have the very last keystroke). Button: `disabled={!date || !hasAnyValueEntered || isCompleting || isComplete}`.

**Web** (`trainingProcessing.tsx`): added the missing date check (there wasn't one at all before) plus a `hasAnyValueForDay(dayIndex)` helper reading `watch()` (react-hook-form's own reactive value snapshot — called with no argument so it subscribes to every registered field and re-renders on each keystroke). Button: `disabled={submittedDays[index] || !dates[index] || !hasAnyValueForDay(index)}`; `onSubmitDay`'s own guard extended the same way for defense in depth, matching mobile's `handleComplete`.

Verified live on both platforms against the same real program ("Rak 6-d"), not just read through: on page load every day's button was already disabled (no date entered on either platform). On web, set Day 1's date via a scripted native-input value change (typing into a real `<input type="date">` through browser automation proved too unreliable to drive directly) and confirmed the button *stayed* disabled until an actual value was written into one of the `XXX/YYxZ` exercise inputs — then confirmed it went back to disabled the moment that value was cleared, with the date left intact. On mobile, typed a real date plus one exercise value into Day 1 through the actual UI and watched its Complete button visibly activate while every other day's stayed disabled, exactly matching web. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms.

## Sprint 53 — Page title always shown during loading, matching Complexes (web)

**Context:** on web, Programs and Wiki hid the real page title behind a skeleton placeholder while their initial fetch was in flight (`if (loading) return <ProgramsListSkeleton/>`, `if (!exercises.length) return <ExerciseListSkeleton/>` — both early returns, so the real `<h2>` never rendered until data arrived). Complexes never had this problem: its title renders unconditionally, only the list below it is skeleton-gated. Asked to make all three consistent, with the choice of direction left to me.

**Chose "always show the real title," not "always show a title-shaped skeleton everywhere."** The title text itself (`base.programs`/`training.myPrograms`, `base.Wiki`) is static, already-known translated copy — it was never actually "loading" data, so skeleton-izing it anywhere (including Complexes, which already gets this right) would be manufacturing a fake loading state for something that's synchronously available. Matches Complexes' own already-correct behavior rather than making Complexes worse to match the other two.

`programs.tsx`: dropped the early `if (loading) return <ProgramsListSkeleton/>`; the title and (`isMyProfile`-gated) "+ Create new program" link now render unconditionally, with only the list portion switching between skeleton/empty-state/real list based on `loading`. `wiki.tsx`: same shape — the title renders unconditionally; the search row, muscle-group tabs, and exercise list stay behind `!exercises.length ? <ExerciseListSkeleton/> : (...)`. Both skeletons (`ProgramsListSkeleton`, `ExerciseListSkeleton` in `ui/skeleton/skeleton.tsx`) had their own now-redundant `<span className={styles.title}/>` removed — a real, unconditional title next to a skeleton-shaped fake one would have been the exact "overlap" this sprint was fixing, just relocated rather than resolved.

Mobile's Programs and Wiki screens turned out to already be correct — both already render `<ThemedText type="pageTitle">` unconditionally with no competing title placeholder in `ProgramsListSkeleton`/`WikiSkeleton`, confirmed by reading the source *and* by force-rendering each screen's loading state live (temporary `(true || loadState.state === 'loading')`, screenshotted, then reverted) rather than trusting the code alone. No mobile changes were needed.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on web. Live-verified via the established force-hack-then-revert pattern on both web routes (`(true || loading)` / `(true || !exercises.length)`, screenshotted, reverted, confirmed no leftover hack via `git diff`/grep afterward): "Мої програми" and "Wiki" both now read immediately, with only the content below them showing a skeleton.

## Sprint 54 — Trainer-level badge on the profile page (mobile + web)

**Context:** `profiles.is_trainer` had no visible surface anywhere on the profile page besides gating buttons — no indication that a user *is* a trainer at all. Asked to show it "nicely," with a tiered visual language (Iron/Bronze/Silver/Gold-style ranks) keyed to accepted-client count, and given design latitude on placement and exact styling.

**Design went through a live-reviewed mockup first** (an Artifact comparing 4 placement options and 4 tier-indicator styles, then 2 rounds of tier-color revision from direct feedback) rather than being implemented from a single guess — see the mockup's own history for the earlier "Wood" tier attempt (rejected: broke the "precious metal" story even with a distinct color) before landing on a recolored dark-graphite Iron, separated from Silver by brightness/saturation rather than hue alone. Final decisions: a dedicated badge row (icon + "Trainer · {tier}" + client count) placed right after city/date, before the friends-count section — not an inline chip next to the name (crowds long names) or an avatar-corner emblem (covers the face on a 64–96px avatar). Thresholds: 1–5 Iron, 6–10 Bronze, 11–15 Silver, 16+ Gold; `0` clients hides the block entirely, matching `PeoplePreview`'s own "no empty state" rule.

**New RPC** (`supabase/migrations/20260819071327_add_trainer_client_count_rpc.sql`): `get_trainer_client_count(target_trainer_id)`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, returns only a count. Needed because `trainer_clients`' SELECT RLS policy (`trainer_id = auth.uid() or client_id = auth.uid()`) only lets a user read relationships they're personally part of — a plain query can't see another trainer's client list at all, so a badge on someone *else's* profile would otherwise be permanently empty. The RPC deliberately returns an aggregate only, never the underlying rows, so it can't be used to learn who a trainer's clients actually are — verified live via `supabase db query` both as the privileged agent role and with `set local role authenticated` (no matching `auth.uid()`), confirming the bypass works for the count without needing RLS-bypassing row access.

Tier colors added as first-class theme tokens on both platforms (`tierIron`/`tierBronze`/`tierSilver`/`tierGold` + `*Tint` in mobile's `constants/theme.ts`, matching `--tier-*` custom properties in web's `ui/variables.scss`) — not scoped to the badge component alone, following the same pattern every other semantic color in this app already uses. Mobile's `TrainerBadge` (`components/trainer-badge.tsx`) and web's `TrainerBadge`/`TrainerBadge.module.scss` share the same medal SVG path data (two ribbon triangles + a ringed disc with a center dot) so the icon matches pixel-for-pixel across platforms, same as every other cross-ported icon this session.

**A real layout bug caught live, not by inspection:** web's first version used `display: inline-flex` on the badge's own wrapper. Test Tester's profile (no city set) showed the badge overlapping the joined-date line instead of sitting on its own row — the plain `<span>` date has no block-level break of its own (city's span does, via Sprint 49's own fix, but city is optional), so an inline-flex sibling right after it just continues the same inline flow. Fixed by switching to `display: flex` (block-level outer display) with `width: fit-content` replacing the now-meaningless `align-self: flex-start`. Mobile never had this bug class — RN Yoga lays out `View` children in a column by default regardless of any individual child's own display mode, confirmed by mobile rendering correctly on the very first pass.

Own profile reuses `people.length` from the existing "my accepted clients" derivation (`profile/index.tsx`, already fetched for `PeoplePreview`) rather than an extra RPC call — that array is already exactly the client list whenever `is_trainer` is true. Viewing someone else's profile (mobile `profile/[id].tsx`, web `Profile.client.tsx`) calls the new RPC instead, since RLS blocks the direct-query path there.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean on both platforms (same one pre-existing, unrelated mobile error). `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 229 base keys — Ukrainian/Russian/Polish got full `_one/_few/_many/_other` plural forms for the client-count string (matching this app's own existing `daysPart_*` precedent for those three languages), English kept `_one/_other` only, matching that same precedent. Web has no real pluralization system at all (confirmed by its own existing strings, e.g. "Friend Requests ({value})") — its client-count string is a single non-pluralized `{value} clients` per locale, consistent with that platform's own established simplification.

**Live-verified end to end**, not just read through: seeded one real `trainer_clients` row (Test Tester as trainer, Yurii as client, `status = 'accepted'`) directly via SQL — no accepted relationships existed anywhere in the database before this, so there was nothing to demonstrate the feature against otherwise. Confirmed on both platforms: Test Tester's profile shows "Тренер · Залізо" / "1 підопічних" (mobile: "Trainer · Iron" / "1 client") in the correct position; Yurii's own (non-trainer) profile shows nothing in that spot, no leftover gap. This seeded row was left in place (not rolled back) so the user could immediately see and interact with a real badge for their own live testing, as they'd explicitly asked to do next — flagged to them directly rather than left as a silent side effect.

**Same-session follow-up: badge shrunk to one line.** Live feedback on the just-shipped badge: too large, visually heavy next to the rest of the profile header. The two-line layout (bold "Trainer · Tier" title, dimmer client-count line below) collapsed into one compact line — icon down from 22px to 14px, padding tightened, font down to 12px/600-weight — on both platforms identically. Verified live: the badge now reads as a small pill in proportion with the buttons below it rather than a card-sized block.

## Sprint 55 — "Your trainer"/"Your client" button relabeled as an action (mobile + web)

**Context:** the button shown on an accepted trainer/client relationship's own profile page passively stated the relationship ("Ваш тренер"/"Your trainer", or the reverse "Ваш клієнт"/"Your client" depending on which side the viewer is on) despite actually performing a destructive action on tap (opens the remove-confirmation dialog) — pointed out directly against a screenshot of Test Tester's profile, asking for it to read as an action instead, matching the existing "Видалити друга"/"Remove friend" button's convention.

**Decision:** renamed both the key and the value on both platforms rather than just the displayed text, since a key literally named `yourTrainer` holding "Remove trainer" would mislead the next person reading it — mobile's `trainer.yourTrainer`/`trainer.yourClient` (`src/lib/i18n/locales/*.json`) became `trainer.removeTrainer`/`trainer.removeClient`; web's `base.yourTrainer`/`base.yourClient` (`store/language-slice.ts` + `lib/languages.ts`) became `base.removeTrainer`/`base.removeClient`. Confirmed via grep each was used at exactly one call site per platform (mobile: `profile/[id].tsx`; web: `Profile.client.tsx`) before renaming, so no other consumer was silently broken. Translations: EN "Remove trainer"/"Remove client", UK "Видалити тренера"/"Видалити клієнта", RU "Удалить тренера"/"Удалить клиента", PL "Usuń trenera"/"Usuń klienta". Mobile's button already rendered with `themeColor="danger"` (red) beforehand — only the copy needed to change there; web's button uses the same plain `className="button"` the "Remove friend" button next to it does, so no styling change was needed on that platform either.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms, `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 229 keys.

## Sprint 56 — Program cards: the Sprint 45 "Elevated" variant restored as a shared `ElevatedCard`, extended to web, with a dark-theme fix (mobile + web)

**Context:** Sprint 45 had already settled on dropping `ProgramCard`'s border for a shadow-lifted surface (mobile-only, chosen from 5 side-by-side variants). That decision didn't survive the later AppShell/floating-glass rebuild — `program-card.tsx` was rebuilt back to the bordered "Outlined" look at some point during that arc, undocumented as a deliberate reversion (most likely swept up unintentionally alongside the shell's own border-language changes). Asked to bring the elevated look back, this time also on web (which had never had it — its `ProgramItem`/`.programItem` was always bordered), scoped for now to only the My Programs list (not Complexes, not another user's profile programs list), and built as a genuinely shared component this time, since it's expected to be reused by other cards later.

**Design iteration:** an Artifact mockup compared the previously-approved light-theme recipe against three dark-theme options (fill-only lift, fill + top highlight, fill + brand-navy glow) — a plain black shadow doesn't read against an already-dark background, the same conclusion `ProgramCard`'s own pre-existing code comment had already reached. First pass reused `backgroundElement`'s dark value (`#212225`, a neutral grey) as the fill and was rejected live ("колір якийсь темний і не гарний") — the grey clashed with the app's blue-toned dark palette. Fixed by picking a fill in the same navy hue family as `background`/`border` instead, just lighter (`#1B2A47`), then re-comparing the three accent options against that corrected fill. User picked **C — fill + brand-navy glow**.

**Decision — final recipe:** new `elevatedBg` token added to both platforms' palettes (mobile `constants/theme.ts`: light `#F0F0F3` / dark `#1B2A47`; web `ui/variables.scss`: `--elevated-bg`, same values) — light reuses `backgroundElement`'s existing value under its own semantic name (dark deliberately does not, per above). Light: filled + `shadowColor:'#000', shadowOpacity:0.12, shadowRadius:5, shadowOffset:{0,2}, elevation:3` (the exact 8px-list-gap-fit recipe Sprint 45 already tuned). Dark: filled + a 1px `rgba(70,120,190,0.3)` ring + `shadowColor:'#204879', shadowOpacity:0.45, shadowRadius:7, shadowOffset:{0,4}, elevation:6` — the ring/glow color is the same navy as `theme.border`/`--border-color`, tying the "lift" back to the brand accent rather than a generic grey.

**Decision — shared component, not inlined per consumer:** mobile gets a new `src/components/elevated-card.tsx` (`ElevatedCard`, a `View` wrapper resolving light/dark itself via `resolveEffectiveScheme`) — `ProgramCard` gained a `variant?: 'outlined' | 'elevated'` prop (default `'outlined'`, preserving Complexes' and another user's profile programs list unchanged) that swaps its outer surface for `ElevatedCard` when set; `ProgramsList` forwards the same prop; only `programs/index.tsx` (My Programs) passes `variant="elevated"`. Web gets `ui/elevatedCard/elevatedCard.module.scss` (a composable `.elevated` class, not a wrapper component — `ProgramItem`'s root must stay an `<a>` for routing, so a div-wrapper wasn't the right shape here and wasn't built, matching this project's standing anti-premature-abstraction bar) — `ProgramItem` gained the same `variant` prop, composing `.elevated` (plus a `.programItemElevated` override that cancels `.programItem`'s own border) only when set; `programs.tsx` passes `variant={isMyProfile ? 'elevated' : 'outlined'}`, since that one component/route already serves both "my programs" and "another user's programs" depending on the URL's `id` param.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean on both platforms (same one pre-existing, unrelated mobile error).
