Live-verified on web via Chrome automation, logged in, dark theme, at `/training/[own-id]`: computed styles confirmed `background-color: rgb(27, 42, 71)` and `box-shadow: rgba(70,120,190,0.3) 0 0 0 1px, rgba(32,72,121,0.45) 0 4px 14px` on the rendered card, and Test Tester's own programs list (viewed via `/training/[their-id]`) confirmed still plain-bordered, unaffected. Mobile wasn't live-verified this session — no reachable logged-in web-preview session was available at the time — correctness rests on `ElevatedCard` reusing the exact recipe already live-verified on web plus `tsc`/lint passing; flagged directly rather than silently assumed.

## Sprint 57 — Friends list: elevated cards, a smaller photo, and search (mobile + web)

**Context:** asked, against a screenshot of the (own) Friends screen, to put each friend row in "the same blocks we just made" (Sprint 56's `ElevatedCard`/`elevated` recipe), shrink the row a bit — the photo especially — and add search, on both platforms. Confirmed live that both platforms' Friends lists had the same un-treated look Programs had before Sprint 56: mobile's `UserCard` had gone through the identical Sprint 45→AppShell-rebuild reversion story `ProgramCard` had (a same-session Sprint 45 extension had given it the scaled shadow recipe; current code was back to plain "Outlined" with no record of when/why); web's `AllFriends`/`allFriends.module.scss` had never had any card treatment at all — bare flex rows with an 80×80 image, no fill, no border.

**Decision — reused Sprint 56's `ElevatedCard` exactly, no new recipe:** mobile's `UserCard` (`components/user-card.tsx`) gained the same `variant?: 'outlined' | 'elevated'` prop `ProgramCard` has (default `'outlined'`, so Requests/Users stay on the existing bordered look) plus a new `avatarSize?: number` prop (default 40, unchanged for Requests/Users) — `friends.tsx` passes `variant="elevated"` and `avatarSize={32}` at both its `UserCard` call sites (the read-only branch for someone else's friends list, and the own-list branch with the "Remove friend" action). The elevated variant also uses tighter padding (`Spacing.two` vs `card`'s `Spacing.three`) — asked for a visibly more compact row, not just a smaller photo alone. Web's `AllFriends` composes the same `ui/elevatedCard/elevatedCard.module.scss` `.elevated` class `ProgramItem` does (its `friendList__link` stays an `<a>`, so a wrapper component still isn't the right shape, same reasoning as Sprint 56); the photo shrank from 80px to 48px and gained `border-radius: 50%` (previously square — an unintentional inconsistency with mobile's always-circular `Avatar`, corrected as part of this pass rather than left mismatched) and `object-fit: cover`.

**Decision — search mirrors the Users screen's own pattern exactly, not a new one:** mobile's `friends.tsx` gained the same `SearchBar` + `searchQuery` state + `trimmedQuery` + `useMemo`-filtered list shape `users.tsx` already established, filtering by `profile.username` substring (case-insensitive); new `profile.friends.searchPlaceholder`/`noMatch` i18n keys (not reusing `users.searchPlaceholder`, since "Search users" reads wrong on the Friends page) across all four locales. Web's `AllFriends` gained the same `searchRow`/`searchInput`/`searchClear` markup and filtering logic `userList.tsx` already has for Users, duplicated into `allFriends.module.scss` rather than shared (matches this project's existing per-page duplication of that exact block, not a new pattern) — plus new `base.searchFriends`/`noFriendsMatch` keys in both `store/language-slice.ts` and `lib/languages.ts`'s four language blocks. Search only renders when the list is non-empty (`friends.length > 0`), same gating `users.tsx`/`userList.tsx` already use.

## Sprint 58 — Elevated dark recipe retuned; Friends card matched to Programs' own exact metrics (web)

Mobile's `shadowRadius` (RN has no separate CSS-blur equivalent) scaled from 7 to 4 using the same roughly-half-of-CSS-blur ratio already established for the light variant, and `elevation` (Android) scaled from 6 to 4 to match the now-smaller blur radius rather than left disproportionate. Live-verified on web via computed-style inspection (`box-shadow` read back exactly as set).

Fixed by deriving the photo size the same way the padding already matched: `ProgramItem`'s real total height (50px) minus its own padding (8px × 2) gives a 34px content height, so the Friends photo became 34px (was 48px) and `.friendList__link`'s radius dropped to 4px (was 8px) — confirmed via live inspection landing at exactly 50px/4px afterward, not approximately.

**Explicitly NOT mirrored onto mobile, corrected by the user:** initially reasoned mobile didn't have the same 64-vs-50 mismatch (its own Friends/Programs cards already share `Spacing.two` padding, `Spacing.two` list gap, and `Spacing.one` radius via `ElevatedCard`, and mobile's Friends card was already *smaller* than its Programs card, not bigger) — so no mobile change was made. The user corrected this on principle, independent of whether mobile's specific numbers happened to already line up: "ми все робимо і для веб і для моб" (we do everything for both web and mobile) — saved as a reinforcement to [[feedback_web_mobile_parity]], since a platform-specific root cause isn't sufficient grounds to skip the other platform by default.

## Sprint 59 — Complexes accordions get the same elevated block (mobile + web)

**Context:** asked to bring Complexes' cards into the same elevated treatment as Programs/Friends — explicitly *not* forced to the same 50px height (Complexes' accordion rows carry more content per row: title + type/level line, and an expandable day/exercise list), but border-radius and list gap must match exactly.

**Decision — mobile:** `GlobalProgramAccordion`'s outer `ThemedView` (bordered, `Spacing.two`/8px radius) became `ElevatedCard` (no `style` override — its own base radius, `Spacing.one`/4px, already matches `ProgramCard`/`UserCard`'s elevated variant, and `styles.list`'s `gap: Spacing.two` already matched `ProgramsList`/`friends.tsx`'s own list gap, so neither needed touching). `overflow:'hidden'` (needed so the header/expanded-content's square edges don't flatten the card's rounded corners) moved off `ElevatedCard` itself onto a new inner plain `View` (`cardClip`, radius `Spacing.one` again) wrapping the header/content — putting it directly on `ElevatedCard` would have clipped its own shadow/glow away along with the content, since RN clips a view's shadow together with everything else inside it. Three inner `ThemedView`s (`cardHeaderText`, `dayBlock`, `exerciseRow`) picked up an explicit `backgroundColor:'transparent'` — previously invisible against the old bordered card's page-background fill, they'd otherwise show as mismatched opaque rectangles now that the outer surface is `theme.elevatedBg` instead, the same nested-opaque-fill bug class already fixed elsewhere in this codebase (Sprint 40, Home's own cards).

**Decision — web:** the fill/border/shadow moved from a hardcoded `border:"1px solid #204879"`/`boxShadow:"none"` in `Accordion`'s `sx` prop to the same `elevatedBg`/glow values as literals branched on the existing `isDark` boolean (not composing `ui/elevatedCard`'s CSS-module class directly — MUI's own `sx`-generated styles would have fought a composed class for the same properties at matching specificity, tie-broken by unreliable injection order). `complexes.module.scss`'s `.programCard` radius dropped `8px → 4px` and padding `12px → 8px`, matching `ProgramItem`/`AllFriends` exactly. The per-item `margin: "0 0 10px 0 !important"` was removed entirely — `.programList`'s own `row-gap: 8px` already provided list spacing, so the margin was adding a redundant ~18px effective gap on top of it, not something the user asked to keep.

**Bug found and fixed live, not assumed away:** with the explicit border-radius removed from the deprecated bordered look, MUI's own `.MuiAccordion-rounded` class only rounds the *first* item's top corners and the *last* item's bottom corners (all middle items render square-cornered) — built for a flush, gap-free stack of accordions, the opposite of this list's real `row-gap`-separated cards. Caught via direct `getComputedStyle` inspection (`"4px 4px 0px 0px"` / `"0px 0px 4px 4px"` on the two live items), not by eye. Fixed with `borderRadius: "4px !important"` inside the same `sx` object — the built-in rule is more specific than a plain `sx` value, so nothing short of `!important` reliably wins there.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` clean on both platforms (same one pre-existing, unrelated mobile error). Live-verified on web via Chrome automation, dark theme, at `/training/complexes`: both cards render with uniform 4px corners, the correct fill/glow (`getComputedStyle` confirmed `rgb(27, 42, 71)` background and the exact retuned box-shadow), zero extra margin, and expanding a card (verified live, not just inspected) shows the day/exercise list and the danger-bordered "Remove from my programs" button with corners still intact. Light theme wasn't live-toggled for this specific component (its colors are set via the existing `isDark` Redux boolean, not the `data-theme` CSS attribute the earlier force-hack pattern flips, so that shortcut doesn't apply here) — correctness rests on the same `isDark` conditional already proven live elsewhere in this exact file (text/icon color) plus the literal light-mode values matching `ProgramCard`'s own already-verified recipe exactly. Mobile wasn't live-verified this session (no reachable logged-in web-preview session) — `tsc`/lint clean plus reuse of the already-verified `ElevatedCard`/nested-transparent-fill pattern is what correctness rests on there.

## Sprint 64 — In-app account deletion (mobile + web)

**Context:** asked directly whether the app was release-ready for Google Play; flagged two real gaps — no privacy policy, and no in-app account/data deletion (Google requires the latter for any app supporting account creation, not just a support-email request). Explicit follow-up: build the deletion feature now, on both platforms. Went through plan mode given the scope (new DB function, auth deletion, two app codebases) — plan approved before any code was written.

**Database research, done and verified live before writing the function** (`supabase db query --linked --agent=yes`, rolled-back transactions throughout): the authoritative FK picture came from `pg_constraint` directly, not `information_schema` — the latter's `constraint_column_usage`/`table_constraints` join silently omitted every FK actually touching `auth.users` (a real gap in the standard SQL views for this schema, caught only by cross-checking `pg_constraint`). The real picture: `profiles.id → auth.users.id` is NO ACTION (profiles must go first); `programs.user_id → auth.users.id` is CASCADE; `programs.author_id → profiles.id` is NO ACTION on a `NOT NULL` column (no automatic fallback); `training_history.user_id` carries *two* FKs on the same column (CASCADE from `profiles`, NO ACTION from `auth.users` directly); `exercise_logs`/`exercise_drafts.user_id → auth.users` are CASCADE; `friends`/`trainer_clients → profiles` are CASCADE; `global_programs.author_id → profiles` is SET NULL (existing, correct — shared content survives its author's deletion, left untouched).

**The one real subtlety, confirmed against live data before coding around it:** `programs.author_id` can legitimately differ from `programs.user_id` — a real row exists from this session's own trainer-edit testing (Test Tester's program, `author_id` = the trainer who edited it). Since that column is `NOT NULL`/NO ACTION, naively deleting "everything the departing user authored" would delete a program someone *else* still owns, just because the departing user once edited it as their trainer. Fix: reassign `author_id` back to `user_id` for rows merely authored-for-another, then delete only rows actually owned (`user_id = them`).

**Decision — one new SECURITY DEFINER RPC**, following the exact pattern `get_trainer_client_count`/`nearest_city` already established: `supabase/migrations/20260821091120_add_delete_own_account_rpc.sql`'s `delete_own_account()` takes **no parameters** — scoped entirely to `auth.uid()`, so there is no id a caller could ever pass to delete someone else. Sequence: reassign `author_id` → delete owned `programs` (cascades days/exercises/logs/drafts) → delete `profiles` (cascades friends/trainer_clients/training_history) → delete `auth.users`. Granted to `authenticated` only, not `anon`.

**Verified twice, live, before any app code was written** — not assumed from reading the SQL: (1) the raw DELETE sequence run directly as the privileged role against a disposable test `auth.users` row + a trainer-edited-program simulation + a friendship + a training-history row, all in a rolled-back transaction — reassignment, cascades, and final `auth.users` removal all confirmed correct; (2) the *actual deployed function*, called via `set local role authenticated; set local request.jwt.claims = ...` impersonation (the same mechanism a real client call uses to resolve `auth.uid()`), same result; (3) confirmed `anon` role gets a clean "Not authenticated" exception, not a silent no-op or a leak.

**App layer, both platforms mirror their own existing conventions exactly, not new ones:** mobile's `src/lib/supabase/account.ts` (`deleteOwnAccount()`, throw-on-error like every other RPC wrapper in this codebase) vs. web's `lib/accountData.ts` (boolean-return + `console.error`, matching `lib/trainerClientData.ts`'s own established idiom, not the mobile shape). Settings screens on both platforms gained a new button placed directly above the existing Sign Out button — deliberately an *outlined*, not filled, danger button (mobile: `theme.danger` border only; web: `.deleteAccount` with `border: 2px solid var(--danger)`, `background: transparent`) — Sign Out is the common, reversible, instant action and stays filled/prominent; this is the rare, irreversible one and stays visually quieter while still discoverable. Opens the same themed confirm dialog each platform already uses for destructive actions (`ConfirmDialog` on mobile — the Friends "Remove friend" one; `GlobalPopup` on web — the same one used for friend/trainer removal), not a new dialog component. On confirm: call the RPC, then `signOut()` — no manual navigation on mobile (the root layout's session-gated routing already redirects once the session clears, same as the existing Sign Out button); web explicitly `router.push('/')` after, matching its own existing `handleSignOut` shape exactly.

`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx expo lint` clean on both platforms, `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 234 keys. Live-verified via Chrome automation on web: the button renders in the right place/style, clicking it opens `GlobalPopup` with the correct title/warning copy, and Cancel closes it cleanly with no side effects — confirmed via DOM inspection, not just a screenshot. **Deliberately not live-tested end to end** (create a disposable account, actually click Confirm, verify the account is really gone) — that step touches the live production database with an irreversible action beyond a rolled-back transaction, so it was surfaced directly rather than done unilaterally; the user opted to run that final click-through themselves rather than have a throwaway account created and destroyed against the live project during this session.

## Sprint 65 — Registration silently "succeeded" for an already-registered email; genuinely new signups got no feedback either (mobile + web)

**Context:** the user's own attempt to register a disposable test account (to try the just-built account-deletion flow) surfaced two symptoms: "Invalid login credentials" right after a registration that showed no error, and a request that registration auto-sign-in the user instead of requiring a separate login step.

**Root cause, found via live `auth.users` inspection, not guessed:** the email used (`y.dzoma@avivi.com.ua`) already had a real, confirmed account — created via Google OAuth back in 2025-05, not a throwaway. Supabase's `signUp()` deliberately never returns an error for an email that already belongs to a confirmed account (anti-account-enumeration — otherwise an attacker could probe which emails are registered). Both platforms' registration code only checked `error`/`data.session === null` and treated that as "success, needs email confirmation" — so an existing user retrying signup with their own email landed on a false "check your email" success, with **no confirmation email actually sent** for that case, then predictably couldn't log in. Confirmed project-wide, not just this one account: of 7 email/password users, 2 are still stuck permanently unconfirmed — real signups nobody ever told to check their email correctly, or that never got a real confirmation link.

**Fix — detect the collision via the documented `identities` trick, both platforms:** after `signUp()`, `data.user.identities` is an *empty* array specifically for the "email already belongs to a confirmed account" case (a genuinely new signup's `identities` has exactly one entry) — this is the one reliable client-side signal Supabase provides for this case. Mobile's `signUpWithPassword` (`src/lib/supabase/auth.ts`) now returns a 3-way `SignUpResult` (`'signed-in' | 'confirmation-required' | 'already-registered'`) instead of a single boolean; `register-form.tsx` branches on all three, with a new `alreadyRegisteredError` i18n key for the third. Web's `registerUserWithEmail` (`lib/userData.ts`) mirrors the same 3-way `status`; `signInForm.tsx` — which previously just called `router.push('/')` unconditionally on anything that wasn't an explicit `error` — now branches the same way, adding a "check your email" inline message for the `confirmation-required` case (web had no equivalent screen at all before this; mobile already had a full `/confirm-email` route with a resend button, untouched here) and the new `alreadyRegisteredError` message (shown as a field-level error under Email, matching how "Invalid login credentials" already renders on both platforms).

**The "auto-login immediately" half of the request has a real, separate ceiling, surfaced rather than worked around:** for a genuinely new email, `signUp()` only returns a session (enabling true instant auto-login) when the project's Auth "Confirm email" requirement is off. This project currently requires it (confirmed live — real accounts are stuck unconfirmed because of it). Toggling that is a Supabase Auth *project setting*, not a database change — out of reach of the `db query`/migration mechanism used everywhere else this session, and deliberately not attempted via `supabase config push` either: that command replaces the *entire* remote Auth config with whatever's in a local `config.toml`, and no accurate local mirror of the current config exists (Google OAuth client credentials, redirect URLs, SMTP settings, etc.) — pushing an incomplete file risked silently breaking Google sign-in or other working settings project-wide, for every real user, not just this feature. Left as an explicit decision for the user to make directly in the Supabase Dashboard (Authentication → Providers → Email → "Confirm email"), with the tradeoff named (no confirmation = no protection against spam/fake-email signups). Once decided either way, the code fix above already handles both outcomes correctly — immediate sign-in if confirmation is turned off, a correct "check your email" message if it stays on.

**Verified live**, not just read through: reproduced the user's exact reported bug first (registering with the same already-Google-linked email) — before the fix this silently redirected; after, it now shows "Ця пошта вже зареєстрована. Спробуйте увійти замість реєстрації." inline, confirmed via screenshot. Also verified the `confirmation-required` branch with a genuinely new disposable email (`@mailinator.com` — `@example.com` was rejected outright by Supabase's own domain blocklist, a useful confirmation that domain validation happens server-side) — correctly showed the new "check your email" message and did not redirect. The disposable test account created for that second check was cleaned up directly via SQL afterward (`delete from profiles ...; delete from auth.users ...`, the same order this session's own `delete_own_account` RPC uses), not left behind. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms, `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 235 keys.

**Same-session cleanup, explicitly requested:** the user's own `y.dzoma@avivi.com.ua` test account (the one that surfaced this whole bug) was deleted directly via SQL, following the exact sequence `delete_own_account` uses — checked first that it owned nothing (0 programs/friends/trainer links/history), then removed. Also asked to turn off the project's "Confirm email" requirement — deliberately not done via `supabase config push` (no local `config.toml` exists at all in this repo, so pushing one would replace the *entire* remote Auth config with schema defaults, risking Google OAuth/redirect/SMTP settings for every real user) and no direct Management API token was available for a targeted single-field PATCH either. Left as a one-click Dashboard change for the user to make themselves (Authentication → Providers → Email → "Confirm email") rather than attempted through a mechanism that could silently break other production settings.

## Sprint 66 — Registration's First/Last name fields rejected Cyrillic (mobile + web)

**Context:** live on the very next registration attempt after the previous sprint's fixes — Cyrillic first/last names ("тест"/"видалення") were rejected with "First/Last name is not valid".

**Root cause:** both platforms' name validation used an ASCII-only pattern, `/^[A-Za-z]+$/i` — web's `firstNameOptions`/`lastNameOptions` (`lib/validations.tsx`) and mobile's `createNameSchema` (`src/lib/validation.ts`, whose own doc comment already noted it mirrors web's rule exactly) both had it, so this was never a platform-specific gap. A third, already-correct pattern existed in the same web file (`nameOptions`, used for program titles): `/^(?!\d+$)[\p{L}\d\s'-]+$/u` — `\p{L}` matches a letter in *any* script, not just Latin, proving Unicode-aware validation was already an established, working pattern in this codebase, just never applied to person names.

**Decision:** both name validators now use `/^[\p{L}\s'-]+$/u` — same `\p{L}` approach as `nameOptions`, minus the digit allowance (a program title can contain a number, a person's name shouldn't), keeping space/apostrophe/hyphen for multi-word and hyphenated names. `firstNameOptions`/`lastNameOptions` are also consumed by `app/settings/components/profileSettings.tsx` (the Settings name-edit form), so that gets the same fix for free without a separate change.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms. Live-verified on web: resubmitted the exact same "тест"/"видаленн­я" values from the bug report — both accepted with no validation error now, confirmed via screenshot; email/password's own independent validations still correctly blocked the actual submission (a deliberately invalid email/short password were used so no real signup request fired during this check).

**Same-session: a manually-created test account and two things it surfaced.** Asked to create a real login/password directly in the database (the shared project's default test mailer had hit its rate limit, blocking further real registrations) — a `delete_own_account` test account was needed to actually click through the just-built deletion flow. Two real issues surfaced by that manual insert, neither hypothetical:

1. **Login failed with "Database error querying schema"** — several `auth.users` token columns (`confirmation_token`, `recovery_token`, `email_change*`, etc.) were left `NULL` by the manual `INSERT`; GoTrue's own Go code scans these as non-nullable strings, and a `NULL` there breaks its internal query. Fixed by `coalesce`-ing them to `''` on the existing row. Specific to hand-rolled test inserts — the app's own real `signUp()` path already populates these correctly, not a bug in any app code.
2. **A real robustness gap, not just a test-data artifact:** the newly-working account then broke `Profile.client.tsx` with a Next.js `Image` error — `avatar_url` was `null` (the manual insert didn't set one), and the component passed it straight to `src` with no fallback. `ProfileType.avatar_url` is typed as a plain `string`, but the actual database column is nullable, so this could genuinely happen to a real profile too, not just a hand-inserted test one. Fixed with the same `ui-avatars.com` initials-image fallback `registerUserWithEmail` already generates at signup time (`src={profile.avatar_url || `https://ui-avatars.com/api/?name=...`}`) rather than inventing a new fallback mechanism. Mobile's own `Avatar` component already had an initials-fallback for a missing `uri` — this brings web's own profile header in line with behavior mobile already had, not a new capability. Noted in passing, not fixed (out of scope, pre-existing, unrelated to this session's own changes): `Friends.tsx`/`TrainerClients.tsx` already reference a `/avatar-placeholder.png` that doesn't exist anywhere in `public/` — a latent broken-image bug from before this session.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on web.

## Sprint 67 — Program creation wizard: 5-step stepper matching web, Workspace/ScreenLayout cleanup (mobile)

**Context:** the create/edit Program screen's "days" step doubled as both the days-count picker and the exercise-per-day assignment UI in one step, with no progress indicator — web's own `/training/create` flow uses a 5-step MUI `Stepper` (Name/Type/Difficulty/Days/Exercises) with the days-count and exercise-assignment steps kept separate.

**Decision:** new `ProgramWizardStepper` component (`src/components/program-wizard-stepper.tsx`) renders the same 5-step circle-and-connector progress bar, using web's own stock MUI blue (`#1976d2`) rather than mapping it to a semantic theme token — confirmed live that web's `Stepper` has no custom theme applied anywhere in that repo, so the color itself isn't one of this app's own tokens to begin with. `create.tsx`'s step 4 (days) was split into step 4 (days-count only) and a new step 5 (exercise assignment, the content the old step 4 used to also carry), and each step's plain "Back" text link was converted to a full `Button` in a two-button `stepNav` row (spaced with `gap`, matching `confirm-dialog.tsx`'s own two-equal-Button layout) rather than a lopsided text-link-vs-button pairing. A `typeInfo` block (aerobic/anaerobic/crossfit one-line explanations) was added under the Type step, with new `stepper.*`/`typeInfo.*` i18n keys across all four locales.

Two shared components needed a matching change: `Button`'s `filled` variant now stretches full width (`alignSelf: 'stretch'`), matching web's `.submit { width: 100% }` — previously it only matched border/fill/radius/padding, not width. `Workspace` dropped a stray 8px horizontal padding that stacked on top of `.content`'s own 8px inset for a 16px total, double web's actual 8px at its own mobile breakpoint — and the now-fully-superseded `ScreenLayout` component (`scroll`/`contentStyle` roles both already covered by `Workspace`) was deleted rather than left as dead code.

`npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` (same one pre-existing, unrelated `use-color-scheme.web.ts` error), and `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` (243 keys) all clean.

## Sprint 68 — Program cards, create button, and floating header/nav chrome: pixel-parity with web (mobile)

**Context:** asked, against side-by-side screenshots at matched viewports, to bring the Programs list's cards, the "+ Create new program" button's spacing/type, and the floating Header/Navigation/TrainingSubNav bars in line with web — web's pills read narrower with a proper frosted-glass look, mobile's read wider with a flat dark tint. Several of the mismatched values had prior code comments claiming they were already "measured" against web; this session live-measured web's actual `getComputedStyle` output via Chrome automation instead of trusting those comments, and fixed only what genuinely didn't match.

**Decision — ProgramCard:** title/subtitle were using `ThemedText`'s generic `default`/`small` types (16/500/24lh, 14/500/20lh) — web's own mobile-breakpoint reset (`ui/base.scss`) is 14px/400 weight for the title and `.programItem p`'s explicit 12px/400 for the subtitle, both with the browser's natural (`normal`) line-height, live-measured at ~17px/~14px. Set directly as `fontSize`/`fontWeight`/`lineHeight` on `ProgramCard`'s own `title`/`subtitle` styles rather than changing the shared `ThemedText` types (reused elsewhere with different, correct sizing). Card height dropped from 66px to 52px, matching web's 50.5px far more closely than before.

**Decision — ElevatedCard:** dark-mode `shadowRadius` was `4`, based on a code comment claiming RN's blur-radius-to-CSS mapping is "roughly halved" from web's `8px` blur — live comparison against the light variant (already `shadowRadius: 5` for web's `5px` blur, a 1:1 match) showed that assumption was wrong; the dark glow was rendering at half web's actual softness. Corrected to `shadowRadius: 8`.

**Decision — create button/spacing:** `programs/index.tsx`'s `createLink` wrapper had its own `marginBottom: Spacing.three` stacked on top of the parent `Workspace`'s own `contentStyle` gap (also `Spacing.three`), doubling the button-to-list gap to 32px against web's actual 16px — removed the redundant margin. The button's label was `type="smallBold"` (700 weight); web's `.createButton span` is `font-weight: 500` at the same 14px — switched to `type="small"` (14/500), an exact match.

**Decision — floating chrome (Header/Navigation/TrainingSubNav):** all three shared one `Spacing.three` (16px) constant for both their left/right margin and their top-or-bottom screen-edge offset. Web's own `.header`/`.floatingWrap` rules use two different literal values — 8px sides, 12px top/bottom — so each component's single margin constant was split into a side constant (`Spacing.two`, 8px) and a vertical constant (a new local `12`, matching web's literal, not from the `Spacing` scale). Separately, `expo-blur`'s `BlurView` renders correctly on native but sets its own background/`backdrop-filter` imperatively on the underlying DOM node on web — confirmed live that passing an overriding `style` prop had no effect at all, so its actual web output stayed its own `intensity`-derived ~9px blur plus a flat `rgba(25,25,25,.35)` tint from `tint="dark"`, nowhere near web's `backdrop-filter: blur(32px) saturate(180%)` with no fill. Fixed by skipping `BlurView` entirely on the web platform (`IsWeb`, `constants/theme.ts`) and rendering a plain `View` styled with the exact web values (`WebGlassStyle`) instead — native's already-tuned `BlurView` (`intensity={45}`) is untouched.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx prettier --check` clean on every file touched. Live-verified via Chrome automation against the running web app (`localhost:3000`) and the mobile web preview (`localhost:8081`) at matched viewports throughout — card height/font/line-height, button gap/weight, and floating-bar margin/background/`backdrop-filter` were each confirmed via `getComputedStyle` on both apps before and after, not just visually compared. No web changes were needed anywhere in this sprint — web was already correct in every case checked; mobile was catching up to it.

## Sprint 69 — Program edit wizard: active-step highlight, click-to-jump, and a per-step Save button (mobile + web)

**Context:** asked to make the wizard's stepper show which step is currently active more clearly, let tapping any step jump straight to it, and add a way to save an in-progress edit without walking every remaining step — all against screenshots of the mobile edit flow, applied to both platforms per the standing parity rule.

**Decision — active-step highlight + click-to-jump:** a completed step and the active one shared the exact same fill color on both platforms (web: MUI's own default `StepIcon` look; mobile: `ProgramWizardStepper` copying it) — only the checkmark-vs-number told them apart, easy to miss. Added a soft glow ring unique to the active circle (mobile: `shadowColor`/`shadowRadius`, a new `activeRing` style; web: `box-shadow` on `.MuiStepIcon-root.Mui-active`, same recipe) and bolded the active label. For click-to-jump: mobile wraps each step in a `Pressable` calling a new `onStepPress` prop; web swapped `StepLabel` for `StepButton`. MUI's `Stepper` turned out to silently disable every `StepButton` past the furthest-reached step unless given `nonLinear` — found only by testing the actual click, not by reading the props. Enabling `nonLinear` in turn disabled MUI's own automatic "every earlier index is completed" inference (needed explicit `completed={index + 1 < activeStep}` per `Step`, replicating what linear mode used to infer for free) — first deploy of the click-to-jump fix silently dropped every checkmark on already-passed steps until this was added back.

**Decision — per-step Save button (edit mode only):** every step (1-4; step 5's own "Next" already saves) gained a Save button below the existing Back/Next row — reusing the exact same save path each platform's final step already used (mobile: `handleSaveStructurePress`; web: `handleSave`/`performSave`), same removal-confirmation flow, same navigate-away-on-success. Step 1 needed special handling on both platforms: the typed name only reaches shared state when the step's own form is submitted (Next or Save), so a plain "commit then save" in one click handler would read the *previous* render's stale value — both `handleSaveStructurePress`/`performSave` were given an optional `titleOverride` parameter, threaded from the step 1 form's own just-submitted value rather than read back from state. Web's `performSave` needed the override carried in a `pendingTitle` state value too, since the removal-confirmation popup can interrupt the save with an extra click before it actually runs.

**Two more rounds correcting the Save button's own layout, in direct response to live feedback each time:** (1) first pass squeezed Save into the *same* row as Back/Next as a third button — rejected outright ("this isn't what I asked for, all the buttons should stay, Save goes in a new row below"). (2) Second pass put Save on its own row below, but full-width — rejected as visibly wider than Back/Next's own row. (3) Third pass matched Back/Next's width via an invisible flex spacer, right-aligned under Next — rejected as looking bad (an off-balance orphan with dead space to one side); centered it via a symmetric pair of spacers instead, still rejected. Asked directly rather than guessing a fourth layout, and inspecting the *actual* live cause (not just the symptom) found the real bug: web's shared `.actions` class carried its own `padding: 0 16px` and `margin-top: 16px`, both redundant with — and stacking on top of — the parent step container's own zero padding and existing `row-gap`. That redundant padding was the entire reason Back/Next read narrower than a bare Save button in the first place; removing it from `.actions` (and the same redundant `padding: 0 16px` on `.type__changer`, the Aerobic/Anaerobic/Crossfit pill row) made Back/Next/Save/pills all naturally flush to the same edges, and Save could go back to being a plain full-width button — no spacer hack needed on either platform. Mobile never had this bug (`stepNav`/`stepContent` carry no padding of their own), so its own spacer-View version was reverted the same way once the shared cause was understood, restoring the plain full-width `Button` mobile started with.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms throughout every round; `npx prettier --check`/`--write` clean on every touched mobile file. Live-verified via Chrome automation on both `localhost:3000` and `localhost:8081` at every step (1 through 5, including the untouched step 5 sharing web's same `.actions` class) after the final CSS fix — confirmed no regression from the shared-class edit, not just the two specific steps shown in screenshots.

## Sprint 70 — Exercise-picker popup: header/nav clearance, Save/Create label, and a save-corrupting event bug (web + mobile)

**Context:** three more rounds of live-screenshot feedback on the exercise-picker popup (`exercisesChooser.tsx`, web only — mobile's own equivalent is a full-screen route push, never affected by any of this): the floating header disappeared behind the popup when it opened; once fixed, the search box and muscle-group tabs then disappeared behind the header instead; once that was fixed too, the bottom nav still covered the tail of both scrollable columns and the Cancel/Confirm row after scrolling to the true end.

**Decision — header visibility:** `.header` (`header.module.scss`, mobile breakpoint) was a bare `position: fixed` with no `z-index` — deliberately, per that rule's own long-standing comment, to avoid trapping the Settings dropdown's backdrop below Navigation/TrainingMenu's own `z-index: 20`. That dropdown is now portaled straight to `document.body` (`menu.tsx`), so it can no longer be trapped by anything `.header` does — confirmed by reading `menu.tsx`, not assumed. Gave `.header` the same `z-index: 20` Navigation/TrainingMenu already use, so it now paints above the popup (`z-index: 12`) the same way those two bars already did.

**Decision — content clearance, three iterations:** `.popup` moved its own `top` down to clear the now-higher header (`calc(80px + safe-area-inset-top)`, the app-wide single-floating-bar convention), and `.exercisesList`/`.navCreate` (the muscle-group tab column, `wiki.module.scss`) gained matching `padding-bottom`/height reductions so their own scrollable boxes still fit inside the shorter popup. First pass used the app-wide `80px` bottom clearance — insufficient, because this page (any `/training/*` route) stacks *two* floating bars, TrainingMenu above the main Navigation, not one; `.exercisesList`'s last item and `.navCreate`'s last tab both still landed behind TrainingMenu even at true max scroll (confirmed via `element.scrollTop = element.scrollHeight` plus `getBoundingClientRect()` against each floating bar individually — `document.querySelector('[class*="floatingBar"]')` alone was misleading, since it silently matches whichever of the two same-named-pattern bars is first in DOM order, not necessarily the visually higher one). Corrected to `136px` (TrainingMenu's own `bottom: 76px` + `48px` height + `12px` breathing room, the same total `training.module.scss`'s own `.content` already reserves for in-flow page content — this popup needs the *full* value rather than that file's own incremental `56px`, since being `position: fixed` it inherits no ambient clearance the way normal flow content does). Live-tuned a final round of exact pixel values in DevTools against the real rendered layout (`150px`/`164px` for both scrollable columns, `132px` for the fixed Cancel/Confirm row, `70px` max-width for the tab column after finding its `.tab`/`.tabActive` children's own fixed `64px` width didn't fit the column's original `60px`) — applied verbatim rather than re-derived, since they were confirmed correct by direct inspection.

**Decision — Save/Create label:** ` selectExercisesStep.tsx`'s final button always read "Create", even when editing an existing program — unlike every other in-wizard action, which already switched correctly. Now shows the shared `base.save` ("Save") when `initialProgram` is set, mirroring mobile's own already-correct `isEditMode ? saveChanges : createTitle` switch on the same button.

**A real, severe bug found only by actually clicking Save rather than just screenshotting the layout:** every direct `onClick={onSave}` / `onClick={onNext}` (web, four step components) and the mobile `onPress={handleSaveStructurePress}` on step 5's final button passed the click/press event through as that function's own `titleOverride?: string` argument — `titleOverride ?? programName` treats any object as truthy, so the DOM/gesture event silently became the program's title, which then failed to serialize (a circular-structure JSON error, from the event's own React fiber reference) inside Supabase's update call. This affected program **creation** too, not just editing, since ` selectExercisesStep.tsx`'s final-step button wires to the same `handleSave` unconditionally regardless of mode. Every such call site now wraps in a no-arg arrow function (`onClick={() => onSave()}`), and `handleSave`/`performSave` (web) and `handleSaveStructurePress` (mobile) both gained a `typeof titleOverride === 'string'` guard as defense-in-depth against the same bug class recurring at some future call site — this was only caught because earlier live-verification in this same session happened to always exercise step 1's Save (which commits via `handleSubmit`, a safe pattern) rather than steps 2-5's direct-reference buttons.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms throughout. Live-verified via Chrome automation: header/search box/tabs/Cancel-Confirm all confirmed clear of both floating bars via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` comparison (not just visual screenshots) at a 375×600 viewport; the event-argument bug was reproduced and confirmed fixed by actually clicking Save on step 2 and the final Save/Save-changes button on step 5 on both `localhost:3000` and `localhost:8081`, checking the console for the circular-structure error and confirming the saved program's title/data survived intact.

## Sprint 71 — Program detail's Type/Level/Author block took up too much vertical space (mobile + web)

**Context:** on the program detail screen, the Type/Level/Author info block rendered as three separate stacked lines under the title row, taking up a disproportionate share of the screen on a phone-width viewport before the day/exercise list even started.

**Decision:** both platforms' info block switched from a vertical stack to a wrapping row, right-aligned, so all three fields normally sit on one line and only wrap to a second when the viewport is too narrow. Mobile's `infoBlock` style (`src/app/(app)/programs/[id].tsx`) went from `flexDirection: 'column'` with a small `gap` to `flexDirection: 'row', flexWrap: 'wrap', justifyContent: 'flex-end'`; web's `.detail__info` (`programDetail.module.scss`) got the same `flex-direction: row; flex-wrap: wrap` at its own mobile breakpoint, previously column-only until the existing 768px override.

Per direct follow-up feedback, the row's own gaps were then widened relative to the initial compacting pass (mobile: `columnGap` 16px→24px, `rowGap` 2px→4px; web: `column-gap` 8px→12px, `row-gap` 4px→8px) and the label/value text size dropped to 12px on both platforms (mobile via a new `infoText` style override on each `ThemedText`; web via `font-size: 12px` on `.detail__info`), keeping the row legible at the smaller size it now needs to occupy less width. A final round on web increased `.detail__info`'s own vertical `margin` by 16px (12px→28px) to restore breathing room between the title row and the day-tabs stepper now that the block itself is shorter — mobile's equivalent spacing is already governed by the screen's single `content` gap (`Spacing.four`, 24px, applied uniformly between all top-level blocks) rather than a per-block margin, so no mobile-side change was needed for that last step.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx prettier --check` clean on the touched mobile file. Live-verified via Chrome automation on both `localhost:8081` (mobile web preview) and `localhost:3000` (web) — confirmed the three fields render on one line at normal widths, and confirmed the final web margin change via `getComputedStyle` after a hard reload (the live page had briefly shown a stale `16px` mid-session before the reload, from Turbopack's CSS-module fast refresh not having fully applied an in-progress edit — not a real discrepancy in the committed source).

## Sprint 72 — Added a third Home tab, News: a read-only project-updates feed (mobile + web)

**Context:** requested as a new feature: a third tab alongside the existing Records/Trainings pair (Trainings staying the default/middle tab), showing the project's own update/announcement feed — date+time, an optional image, and a description per entry, e.g. "we added page X — here's why" with a screenshot. Explicitly scoped down by the user before implementation: content is added manually via Supabase (no in-app authoring UI), and every entry renders fully inside its own list card (no separate detail screen).

**Decision — new `news` table, public-read-only:** this repo's first *new-table* migration since the `cities` precedent (`supabase/migrations/20260818110000_add_cities_and_profile_location.sql`) — a fresh `supabase/migrations/20260906120000_add_news.sql` creates `public.news` (`id`, `title`, `description`, `image_url` nullable, `published_at`, `created_at`), RLS enabled with a single permissive `SELECT` policy and deliberately no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy at all, since rows are never written by a client — applied live via the already-linked `supabase db query --file ... --linked` (the CLI is linked to `supabase-corefit-restored`, confirmed via `supabase projects list` before touching anything).

**Decision — mirror the existing Records/Trainings tab machinery exactly, add nothing new to it:** both platforms' tab switcher is already an array-of-`{key, label, href, isActive}` mapped into equal-width pills (mobile: `home-sub-nav.tsx`'s `bar` style already uses `justify-content: space-around`; web: `homeMenu.tsx`'s `.floatingBar`) — proven to already scale cleanly to 3 items by the pre-existing Complexes/Programs/Wiki bar. Added a third `news` entry to each array (mobile: `src/components/home-sub-nav.tsx`; web: `app/components/homeMenu/homeMenu.tsx`'s `ITEM_DEFS`), ordered `records, trainings, news` on both so `trainings` stays visually in the middle, and registered the new route (mobile: `Stack.Screen name="news"` in `(home)/_layout.tsx`; web: `app/(home)/news/page.tsx`). No style changes to either pill bar were needed.

**New screen, mirrors the Trainings feed's own shape, not a new pattern:** mobile's `src/app/(app)/(home)/news.tsx` copies `(home)/index.tsx`'s exact `loadState` discriminated-union / `useFocusEffect` / `Workspace`+`FlatList` / `useHomeChromeClearance` skeleton, swapping the avatar+exercise-list card body for an optional `expo-image` (16:9, `contentFit="cover"`, matching the wiki detail image treatment) + title + date/time (`toLocaleDateString`+`toLocaleTimeString`, device locale, matching Home's own feed style rather than the compact `uk-UA` table style used elsewhere) + description. Web's `app/(home)/news/components/news.tsx` mirrors `records.tsx`'s structure and reuses `trainingHistories.tsx`'s card composition (`.elevated` class from `ui/elevatedCard`), with a plain `<img>` (matching Records' own convention, not `next/image` — this table's images aren't expected to come from a domain in `next.config.js`'s whitelist). Web's loading state is a small new local `NewsCardSkeleton`, not the existing `NewsSkeleton` (`ui/skeleton/skeleton.tsx`) — that component turned out to be an internal-only name for the *Trainings* feed's own skeleton (avatar + exercise-line list), the wrong shape for an actual news card.

**Data layer:** one file per table, per this repo's established convention — mobile's `src/lib/supabase/news.ts` (Zod `newsRowSchema`, `getNewsFeed()`) and web's new `lib/newsData.ts` (`fetchAllNews()`, mirroring `fetchAllTrainingHistories`'s shape). i18n: `components.homeSubNav.news` (tab label) plus a new top-level `news.{loading,empty}` block added to all four mobile locales; web needed the same `news`/`newsEmpty` keys added in **two** places — `lib/languages.ts`'s four language blocks *and* `store/language-slice.ts`'s own separate hard-coded English `initialState.text.base` object, which turned out to be the actual type source for `useAppSelector(getText)` (a pre-existing duplication between the two files, not introduced by this change).

**Known, pre-existing limitation carried over unchanged:** web's `HomeMenu` already returns `null` at desktop width (≥768px) — Records has had no desktop entry point at all since it shipped, only reachable via the narrow floating pill. News has the identical limitation; not introduced here and out of scope to fix.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos; `npx prettier --check`/`--write` clean on touched mobile files; `node scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` clean at 246 keys. Live-verified via Chrome automation on `localhost:8081` (mobile web preview): the News pill renders as the third tab and is selected correctly, two seeded test rows (one with `image_url`, one without) rendered with correct image/title/date+time/description, and the empty state (`t('news.empty')`) rendered correctly after the test rows were deleted. Web's `/news` page content itself (title, both card variants, image, date+time, description) was confirmed correct via the same seeded rows at the default (desktop-width) viewport — this session's Chrome automation could not force an actual sub-768px viewport for the web app specifically (`resize_window` had no effect on `window.innerWidth` for that tab, a tooling limitation, not a code issue), so the floating pill itself was not visually screenshotted on web; it was not re-verified beyond code review, on the strength of being a one-line addition to the same `ITEM_DEFS` array the already-working `records`/`trainings` entries live in. Test rows were deleted from `public.news` after verification.

## Sprint 73 — Two missing-title regressions surfaced by the new News tab: Home's Trainings feed and the bare `/training` redirect skeleton (mobile + web)

**Context:** live screenshots comparing the just-shipped News tab against Wiki/Complexes/Programs surfaced that Home's own Trainings tab — News' immediate sibling — had never had a page title at all, on either platform, and that web's bare `/training` route (a client-side redirect to `/training/{userId}`) showed its `ProgramsListSkeleton` with no title either, unlike the real Programs page it redirects to.

**Decision — Home's Trainings feed gets the same unconditional `pageTitle` every other list screen already has:** mobile's `(home)/index.tsx` had never had one (confirmed missing, unlike `(home)/records.tsx`'s `<ThemedText type="pageTitle">{t('components.homeSubNav.records')}</ThemedText>`, rendered before its own `loadState` branches) — added the equivalent line reusing `t('components.homeSubNav.trainings')`, and removed the now-redundant `marginTop: clearance.top` from the loading/error branches and `paddingTop: clearance.top` from the success `FlatList` (the title itself now absorbs that top clearance, exactly as `records.tsx` already does). The just-added mobile `(home)/news.tsx` got the identical treatment (it should have had this from the start — an oversight in Sprint 72, not a new decision) using `t('components.homeSubNav.news')`. Web's `app/components/news/trainingHistories.tsx` (the Trainings feed's actual component — the file lives in a directory literally called `news`, unrelated to the new News feature) gained `<h2 className="pageTitle">{base.trainings}</h2>` in both its skeleton-return branch and its loaded-content branch — `base.trainings` already existed in `lib/languages.ts` (all four locales) but had never actually been used anywhere in the codebase until now.

**Decision — the bare `/training` redirect skeleton gets a title too, matching where it's headed:** `app/training/page.tsx` (`TrainingRedirect`) resolves the current user's id then `router.replace`s to `/training/{userId}` (the real Programs page, which already shows `training.myPrograms` unconditionally per Wiki/Complexes/Programs' shared convention) — but the redirect's own transient `<ProgramsListSkeleton/>` had no title, a brief but real flash of a title-less skeleton on every visit to bare `/training`. Added `<h2 className="pageTitle">{training.myPrograms}</h2>` above it, always `myPrograms` (never `base.programs`) since this route only ever redirects to the current user's own trainings.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation: mobile's Trainings and News screens both show their title on `localhost:8081` at `/` and `/news`; web's Home page shows "Trainings" above the feed on `localhost:3000/`. The `/training` bare-redirect skeleton itself completes too fast locally (sub-frame) to screenshot the transient title directly — verified by code review only (the same unconditional-title-before-conditional pattern already confirmed live-working on every other screen in this sprint and the prior one).

## Sprint 74 — Per-exercise "sets" config: no longer typed by hand into every logged value (mobile + web)

**Context:** every logged workout value was free text like `"100/8x3"` (weight/reps×sets), retyped in full on every single session. Live-data analysis confirmed the `xN` (sets) part never actually varies for a given exercise-in-a-program across sessions — only weight/reps do — so it was moved to a one-time, per-exercise setting (1-7, default 3) configured in the wizard's "Exercises" step, with the logging input now auto-appending it. The stored value's shape is deliberately unchanged (`"weight/repsxsets"`, still, e.g. `"77/6x3"`) — only how it's produced changed, so the leaderboard RPCs (which only regex the leading digits) and every history display needed zero changes.

**Decision — new `sets` column, and this table's first-ever UPDATE policy:** `supabase/migrations/20260906130000_add_program_exercises_sets.sql` adds `program_exercises.sets` (`smallint not null default 3 check (sets between 1 and 7)`). `program_exercises` previously only had SELECT/INSERT/DELETE policies — edits were modeled as insert-or-delete, never in-place update, specifically because updating a row's `exercise_id` in place risked misattributing another exercise's `exercise_logs`/`training_history`. Changing just `sets` on an already-saved slot doesn't have that risk (the row keeps meaning the same exercise), so two new UPDATE policies were added (owner-based, and the existing trainer-editing-a-client's-program condition), mirroring the exact `EXISTS` conditions the INSERT/DELETE policies already use.

**Decision — the wizard's step-5 "Exercises" summary gets a real per-exercise list, on both platforms:** the user's own screenshot pointed at this exact screen (numbered exercise names, an "Edit Exercises" button per day) — but that shape turned out to be **web-only** going into this sprint (`app/training/create/components/ selectExercisesStep.tsx`'s `SelectExercisesStep`); mobile's equivalent (`src/app/(app)/programs/create.tsx`, step 5) only ever showed a bare "N exercises selected" count with no names, since the wizard's own state never carried resolved exercise names. Brought to parity: mobile's step 5 now resolves names via `getExercises()`/`localizeExercise()` (fetched once on mount) and renders the same per-day numbered list web already had, with a new `SetsStepper` component (`src/components/sets-stepper.tsx`) per row — a compact `−`/`×N`/`+` control, not a native picker, since no picker/select component exists anywhere in this app yet and one wasn't worth adding for a 1-7 range. Web's `SelectExercisesStep` gained a real `<select>` (1-7) per row instead, plus new CSS (`create.module.scss`'s `.exerciseList`/`.exerciseRow`/`.setsSelect`) — the rows had no dedicated spacing before (bare `<ul>/<li>`), which is what the user's own "give the exercises more space so the selects don't collide" ask was about.

**Data layer, both platforms:** mobile's `WizardExerciseSlot` (`program-wizard-store.ts`) and web's `EditableProgramDay.exercises` (`CreateEditProgram.tsx`) both gained a `sets` field alongside `exerciseId`, threaded through: the exercise-picker/`ExercisesChooser` selection flow (preserves each kept exercise's already-configured `sets`, defaults new ones to 3 — mobile's `exercise-picker.tsx` `handleConfirm` and web's `selectExercisesStep.tsx` `handleSelect`, both now building `{exerciseId, sets}` from a previous-slot lookup map rather than bare ids); `createProgram`/`createTrainingProgram` (insert `sets` on every new row); and `updateProgramStructure`/`updateTrainingProgram`, which now also compute a `setsUpdates` diff — for a slot whose `exercise_id` is unchanged but `sets` differs, an actual `.update({sets})` fires against that row's existing id (the new capability above), rather than the delete-then-reinsert every other kind of "removal" already used. `global_program_exercises` (a separate, trainer-authored table) was deliberately left out of scope — has no `sets` column of its own, so copying a global program into a personal one (`addGlobalProgramToUser`, mobile's `complexes.ts`) just defaults every copied exercise to 3, same as any brand-new wizard exercise.

**Logging UI, both platforms:** placeholder changed from `"XXX/YYxZ"` to `"XXX/YY ×{sets}"` (mobile: `workout-log-form.tsx`; web: `trainingProcessing.tsx`), so the configured count is visible without being typeable. The `xN` suffix is appended only at final-save time — mobile's `handleComplete` (via a new `buildValuesWithSets` helper) and web's `completeDay` (`lib/trainingData.ts`, which now also fetches each drafted `program_exercise_id`'s `sets` before building `valuesMap`) — never when a draft is autosaved on blur, so a resumed draft still shows plain "weight/reps" in the input, never something the user didn't type themselves. Both platforms' exercise-list fetch (mobile: `programs/[id].tsx`'s `dayExercises`; web: `ProgramFull`/`fetchProgramDetail`) now also selects `program_exercises.sets` for this purpose.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation against the linked Supabase project throughout, not just the UI: on mobile (`localhost:8081`), opened an existing program's edit wizard, bumped one exercise from ×3 to ×4 via the stepper, saved, and confirmed via `supabase db query --linked` that the *same* `program_exercises.id` now had `sets = 4` (an UPDATE, not a new row) — then logged a workout entry typing only `"42/9"` and confirmed the saved value was `"42/9x4"`. Repeated the equivalent flow on web (`localhost:3000`): changed a `<select>` from ×3 to ×5, saved, confirmed the same in-place UPDATE (and that the exercise's existing training-history entries, keyed by that same `program_exercise_id`, still resolved correctly — proving the row's identity survived the edit), then typed `"77/6"` into the log input and confirmed the stored value was `"77/6x3"` (that exercise's actual configured count) and that the draft itself (read directly from `exercise_drafts`) stayed the raw `"77/6"`, unsuffixed. All test rows/logs and the two sets changes were cleaned up / reverted afterward, leaving the real programs exactly as found.

## Sprint 75 — Logging input's `×N` label moved outside the field, both platforms

**Context:** direct follow-up feedback on Sprint 74 — the `×N` sets hint baked into the placeholder text (`"XXX/YY ×3"`) sat *inside* the input, sharing space with the placeholder itself; the user wanted it moved to a separate label beside the field, 8px gap, per a reference screenshot.

**Decision:** the input's placeholder reverts to plain `"XXX/YY"`; a new sibling label (`×{sets}`) sits to its right, `8px`/`Spacing.two` gap. Web's `trainingProcessing.tsx`: each `<li>` became a flex row (`.valueRow`, `column-gap: 8px`) holding the `<input>` (now `width: auto; flex: 1`) and a new `<span className={styles.setsLabel}>`; `.process`'s fixed column width grew `94px → 126px` to fit the label without cramping the input. Mobile's `workout-log-form.tsx`: the per-exercise input cell became a row (`valueCellRow`, `gap: Spacing.two`) holding the `TextInput` (`flex: 1`) and a sibling `ThemedText` label; `inputColumn`'s fixed width grew `104 → 136`.

**A real RN-Web flexbox bug caught only by live-checking, not just editing and moving on:** the first pass (`flex: 1` alone on the input, no `minWidth`) rendered the "×N" label completely clipped off the right edge of the app's own bounding box — `getBoundingClientRect()` showed the input's actual rendered width (149.7px) *exceeding* its own flex-row container's width (136px). Root cause: a flex item's default minimum size is its content's intrinsic size, not zero — an `<input>`'s browser-default intrinsic width (its `size`-attribute equivalent) is wide enough on its own to blow past the flex-shrink budget unless `min-width: 0` is set explicitly. Added `minWidth: 0` to the input's style (mobile) — web's `.valueRow input` rule already carried `min-width: 0` from the start, so only mobile needed the fix. Also gave the label `flexShrink: 0` (mobile) / uses `flex-shrink: 0` implicitly as a `<span>` (web) so it can never be the one that gets squeezed instead.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation: web (`localhost:3000`) confirmed correct by screenshot on first pass. Mobile (`localhost:8081`) needed the `minWidth: 0` fix above — caught by `getBoundingClientRect()` showing the actual overflow, not just a screenshot — then reconfirmed correct by screenshot after the fix.

Follow-up, same sprint: web's `.process` column width was tuned down further, `126px → 100px`, per direct devtools measurement feedback — reconfirmed live that the `×N` label still doesn't clip at the narrower width.

## Sprint 76 — First real News content: the sets-per-exercise update itself

**Context:** with the News feature (Sprint 72) shipped and content always meant to be added manually, this is the first real (non-test) row in `public.news` — announcing the sets-config feature from Sprints 74-75 to users.

**Decision — new `news-images` Storage bucket:** no bucket existed for News content specifically (`exercise-images` is scoped to the exercise catalog). Created `news-images` (public, same shape as `exercise-images`) via `insert into storage.buckets`, uploaded via `supabase storage cp --experimental` (the CLI's storage-object commands, not used anywhere else in this project before now). No RLS policies were added on `storage.objects` for it — none exist for `exercise-images` either, since a public bucket already serves objects unauthenticated at the Storage API level regardless of table-level RLS.

**Decision — one composite screenshot, not two separate rows:** `news.image_url` is a single field (by design — one row is one announcement); the user supplied two screenshots (the wizard's sets stepper, and the logging screen's `×N` labels). Rather than picking one and losing the other, both were combined into a single side-by-side PNG (Python/Pillow, dark background matching the app's own theme) and uploaded as the one image.

Content (title/description in Ukrainian, matching how this project's users are actually addressed) inserted directly via `supabase db query --linked`, per this table's standing manual-content convention (see Sprint 72) — no code change, no commit needed for the row itself.

Live-verified via Chrome automation on both `localhost:3000` and `localhost:8081`: the new entry renders correctly in the News tab on both platforms — composite image, title, date/time, and full description.

## Sprint 77 — Weight/reps logging input now sanitizes its own characters, both platforms

**Context:** the `"XXX/YY"` weight/reps input (Sprints 74-75) accepted literally any text, since it always had before — sets being the only thing this whole feature moved out of free typing. Requested: restrict it to digits, `/`, and `.` (decimal weights like `"27.5/10"` are real, existing data — confirmed before implementing, not assumed), with `\` and space normalized to `/`, and `,` normalized to `.` (the most likely typo/IME substitutions for the intended character, rather than being stripped as garbage).

**Decision:** a small `sanitizeWeightRepsValue` function, defined locally in each platform's own log-input component (no shared package between the two repos, matching this project's existing convention) — `text.replace(/,/g, '.').replace(/[\\ ]/g, '/').replace(/[^0-9./]/g, '')`. Mobile (`workout-log-form.tsx`): applied inside `handleChangeText`, so `valuesRef`/`values` state only ever holds the sanitized string — the date input (`setDate`) is untouched, this only applies to the per-exercise value fields. Web (`trainingProcessing.tsx`): react-hook-form's `register` doesn't expose a raw onChange to wrap by default, so the sanitizer is passed via `register(id, { onChange: (e) => { e.target.value = sanitizeWeightRepsValue(e.target.value); } })` — mutating `e.target.value` in place before RHF's own internal (uncontrolled-input) state read, the standard way to mask an RHF-registered field without switching it to a controlled input.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation on both `localhost:3000` and `localhost:8081`: typed the deliberately-adversarial string `"ab27\5,8xz"` into the first exercise's input on each platform and confirmed the live field value came out as `"27/5.8"` in both cases (letters stripped, `\`→`/`, `,`→`.`) — read directly via `document.activeElement.value`, not just eyeballed from a screenshot. Any stray `exercise_drafts` rows the test typing autosaved were cleaned up afterward.

## Sprint 78 — Completing a day looked like it didn't refresh the history table

**Context:** the user reported that a training completed on 05.09.26 wasn't showing up in the program page's history columns. Investigated via `supabase db query --linked` first, not assumed: the `training_history` row existed, correctly linked to the right day, correctly ordered first (most recent) by a fresh query — a genuinely fresh page load *did* show it. So the underlying data and fetch/sort logic were never wrong; something about the live page specifically wasn't showing an update that had already happened.

**Root cause, found by reading the actual layout CSS, not guessed:** web's `trainingHistory.module.scss` has exactly one `.trainingHistory { overflow-x: auto; }` container shared across *every day's* history columns (not one per day) — confirmed by grep, not assumed. Completing a day already does trigger an immediate refetch (`onComplete` → `programDetail.tsx`'s `loadAllHistory` → `setHistory`), which does prepend the new column first (most recent) — but a browser never resets an element's own `scrollLeft` just because its content changed. If that shared container had been scrolled right at all (to see an older entry for *any* day, not necessarily the one just completed), the freshly-added leftmost column landed off-screen to the left — the update genuinely happened, it was just scrolled out of view, reading as "didn't update."

**Decision:** reset scroll position to the start whenever the history data changes, on both platforms. Web's `TrainingHistory` (`trainingHistory.tsx`): a `containerRef` on the `.trainingHistory` div, `useEffect(() => containerRef.current?.scrollTo({left: 0}), [history])`. Mobile's `WorkoutLogForm` (`workout-log-form.tsx`) has the analogous issue at a narrower scope — each day's own history is a separate `ScrollView horizontal`, so only that specific day's own prior scroll position could hide its own new column, but the fix is the same shape: a `historyScrollRef`, `useEffect(() => historyScrollRef.current?.scrollTo({x: 0, animated: false}), [history])`.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation on web (`localhost:3000`), reproducing the exact reported symptom first: scrolled the shared history container right (`scrollLeft = 300`, clamped to the container's actual max, 210.5), completed a day via the real UI flow (native input-value setters + dispatched events, then a real `.click()` on the Complete button — confirmed a new `training_history` row landed in the database), and confirmed `scrollLeft` read back as `0` immediately after, with no manual scroll or page reload. The test row was deleted afterward.

## Sprint 79 — History order reversed: most recent entry now reads last, not first

**Context:** direct follow-up to Sprint 78 — having just made the newest entry surface automatically at the *start* of the scroll (leftmost, most-recent-first), the user clarified that's backwards from what they actually want: the most recent training should read as the *last* column, at the end, not the first.

**Decision:** flipped the sort order at the source on both platforms, rather than just reversing the rendered array — mobile's `getTrainingHistoryForProgram` (`training-history.ts`) and web's `fetchTrainingHistory` (`trainingData.ts`) now both `order('date', {ascending: true})` (mobile also `created_at` ascending as the same-date tiebreaker); web's client-side `sortHistoryRecords` (`programDetail.tsx`) got the same ascending flip. Sprint 78's scroll-to-start fix necessarily flips with it — the whole point was surfacing the just-added entry without a manual scroll, and that entry is now the *last* column instead of the first: mobile's `historyScrollRef` now calls `scrollToEnd({animated: false})` instead of `scrollTo({x: 0})`; web's `containerRef` now sets `scrollLeft = scrollWidth` instead of `scrollTo({left: 0})`. Both still fire from the same `useEffect(..., [history])`.

Scope stays exactly what Sprint 78 already established: only the per-program history views (`getTrainingHistoryForProgram`/`fetchTrainingHistory`) — the Home/Trainings global activity feed (`getTrainingHistoryFeed`) is a separate function and was never touched, correctly still most-recent-first for that different context (a feed, not a left-to-right timeline).

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both repos. Live-verified via Chrome automation on both `localhost:3000` and `localhost:8081`: confirmed via `find`/direct DOM inspection that a day with four history entries now reads oldest→newest left to right (e.g. `21.05.26, 25.05.26, 04.06.26, 05.09.26`), with the most recent column visible without any manual scrolling on page load.

## Sprint 80 — Web: each day's history now scrolls independently (was one shared scrollbar for every day)

**Context:** direct follow-up feedback — web's `TrainingHistory` had exactly one `overflow-x: auto` container wrapping *every* day's history columns (confirmed by grep in Sprint 78, the same fact that explained that sprint's bug); scrolling to see an older entry on one day dragged every other day's columns along with it. Mobile never had this problem — `WorkoutLogForm` is instantiated once per day already, each with its own `ScrollView horizontal`, entirely independent by construction.

**Decision:** moved the scroll container down from the single outer `.trainingHistory` wrapper to each day's own `.historyBlock` (`trainingHistory.module.scss`: `overflow-x`/`max-width`/`width` moved from `.trainingHistory` to `.historyBlock`; `.trainingHistory` is now just a plain vertical flex stack). `trainingHistory.tsx`'s single `containerRef` became a `blockRefs` map (`Record<dayId, HTMLDivElement | null>`, populated via a callback ref per day), and the Sprint 79 scroll-to-end effect now loops over every day's own ref and sets its own `scrollLeft = scrollWidth` independently, rather than resetting one shared scroll position.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified via Chrome automation on `localhost:3000`: forced all six days' history blocks to overflow (temporarily narrowed to 150px each) and set only the first day's `scrollLeft`, confirming every other day's block stayed at `0` — genuinely independent scroll state, not just visually separated columns sharing one scroll position.

**Reverted the same sprint, per direct feedback:** the independent-per-day scroll containers made the whole page scroll in unexpected directions in real use (not caught by the narrow synthetic overflow test above, which only checked scroll-state independence, not actual page-level scroll behavior at realistic content widths/viewport sizes). `git revert`ed cleanly (`861f181`) back to Sprint 79's single shared `.trainingHistory` scroll container — the per-day independence request is not implemented; web's history table shares one horizontal scroll across all days again, same as before this sprint.

## Sprint 81 — Web: preloader-vs-content race, and broken home-feed avatars

**Context:** two unrelated reports. (1) the app-wide splash (`StartPreloader`) sometimes visibly disappeared before the home page's own content had actually loaded. (2) avatars on the home Trainings feed showed as broken images.

**Root cause 1, found by reading the actual code rather than guessing — two independent bugs stacking:**
- **Architectural:** `AppShell.tsx`'s `isLoading` gate (which shows `StartPreloader`) governs only auth/session + `fetchUserSettings` resolving, plus a hardcoded `setTimeout(1000ms)` floor — it has zero awareness of the home page's own data. `children` (the whole route tree, home page included) isn't even mounted until `isLoading` flips to `false`, so `TrainingHistories`' own `fetchAllTrainingHistories`/`fetchProgramExerciseMap`/`fetchExercisesByIds` chain can't start until *after* the splash is already gone — an unavoidable second, separate loading stage (`NewsSkeleton`), by design.
- **Actual bug:** both `Preloader.module.scss` and `startPreloader.module.scss` had a CSS-only `animation: fadeOut 0.5s ease-out 2s forwards` baked into `.preloader`, forcing it invisible 2.5s after mount **regardless of React state**. If session/settings resolution took longer than ~2s, the splash vanished via this CSS timer while `isLoading` was still `true` in React and `children` hadn't mounted at all yet — a genuinely blank screen (not even the skeleton), racing against the unrelated JS `setTimeout`.

**Decision:** removed the `fadeOut` animation (and its now-unused `@keyframes`) from both stylesheets — visibility is now controlled exclusively by the `isLoading`/parent-conditional React state that already gates each component, with no second, independent CSS clock able to hide it early. The JS-side 1000ms minimum-display floor in `AppShell.tsx` was left untouched — a deliberate UX choice (avoids a one-frame splash flash), not part of the bug.

**Root cause 2, home-feed avatars:** `trainingHistories.tsx`'s `<img src={entry.profiles.avatar_url} .../>` had no fallback at all for a null/broken `avatar_url` — a plain broken-image icon (with the `alt="avatar"` text bleeding through) rendered instead. `lib/avatarFallback.ts` (`avatarFallbackUrl`) already existed for exactly this — its own doc comment claimed it was "already added to every avatar-rendering call site," but a repo-wide grep for its only export found **zero usages anywhere** — leftover, never-wired code from an earlier, incomplete session (this file was itself only just committed as an untracked leftover in Sprint 76's cleanup, not part of any code path). Wired it into `trainingHistories.tsx`'s avatar `<img>`: used as the initial `src` when `avatar_url` is falsy, and as an `onError` handler for a URL that's present but broken/expired. Scoped to exactly the reported location — the same doc comment lists five other call sites (records, friends, requests, users, trainer clients, nav) that mention this helper but don't use it either; those are a pre-existing latent gap, not touched here since they weren't part of what was reported.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified via Chrome automation on `localhost:3000`: home feed's previously-broken avatars now render as `ui-avatars.com` initials circles ("YD", "OB") for both profiles shown.

## Sprint 82 — Web: home-feed avatars now show real photos, not just initials fallback

**Context:** Sprint 81's fix made the home Trainings feed stop showing broken-image icons, but the user immediately spotted that it was only *masking* a real problem — the bottom nav's own avatar (`FloatingNavigation`, using Next's `<Image>`) correctly showed his real Google photo, while the exact same photo in the Trainings feed (plain `<img>`) fell back to "YD" initials: "чому в навігації в низу мій аватар (фото) завантажилось, а в новинах пише YD?"

**Root cause:** a plain client-side `<img src="https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/...">` fetched Google's avatar CDN directly from the browser, which returned **HTTP 503** for this URL (confirmed via a direct browser-side fetch) — Google's avatar CDN doesn't reliably serve to arbitrary cross-origin `<img>` tags. `FloatingNavigation`'s avatar never hit this because it already used `next/image`, whose `/_next/image?url=...` route fetches the image **server-side** (no CORS/hotlink restrictions apply there) and serves the result back same-origin.

**Decision:** swapped `trainingHistories.tsx`'s avatar `<img>` for `next/image`'s `<Image>` (`lh3.googleusercontent.com` and `ui-avatars.com` were already both in `next.config.js`'s `images.domains` allowlist), mirroring `FloatingNavigation`'s already-working pattern exactly. The `avatarFallbackUrl` `onError` handler is kept as-is for a genuinely missing/deleted `avatar_url`.

**A second anomaly surfaced during verification, investigated and ruled a non-issue:** after the swap, Yurii Dzoma's card showed his real photo, but Олексій Ващук's card showed a tiny generated-looking blue circle with a white "O" — not a real photo, but also not the two-letter `ui-avatars.com` fallback. Verified this wasn't a caching artifact (killed the dev server, cleared `.next/cache/images`, refetched — identical 394-byte result every time) and wasn't Next-side error handling (fetched `lh3.googleusercontent.com`'s URL directly server-side with `curl`, bypassing `/_next/image` entirely — Google's own origin returned the exact same blue-circle-"O" image, uncompressed, HTTP 200). Conclusion: this **is** Олексій Ващук's real avatar — Google generates this exact "colored circle + first initial" placeholder natively for any account that hasn't uploaded a custom profile photo, so his `avatar_url` genuinely points at Google's own default, not a broken fetch.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified via Chrome automation on `localhost:3000`: Yurii Dzoma's card shows his real photo, Олексій Ващук's card shows Google's own default avatar — both now load through the actual `avatar_url`, with the `ui-avatars.com` fallback reserved for a genuinely absent/broken URL.

## Sprint 83 — Create-program button's loading skeleton restored, both platforms

**Context:** user noticed the "+ Create new program" button on the Programs screen no longer showed any placeholder while the list was loading — it's a static, always-known label, but nothing filled its spot during the fetch either, so the screen looked incomplete for that brief window.

**Root cause:** a prior refactor (commit `4234e27`, "page title always shown during loading") deliberately hoisted both the title and the create-button out of the loading gate, reasoning that neither one depends on fetched data. That's still true for the title, but it left the button with no placeholder at all during `loading` — before that refactor, the button's spot had been covered (accidentally, as part of a single all-or-nothing skeleton) by web's `.programList__create` CSS class, which is 40px/60px tall and shaped exactly like the real button, but went unused and orphaned once the refactor split the skeleton into just the list. Confirmed via `grep` it had zero usages anywhere.

**Decision:** put the button back behind its own loading check specifically (title stays unconditional), rendering a matching skeleton bar in its place:
